      "id": "Ptah adalah dewa pencipta Mesir dan pelindung perajin serta arsitek. Dalam triad Memphis, ia menjadi suami Sekhmet.",
      "en": "Ptah is an Egyptian creator god and patron of craftsmen and architects. In the Memphis triad he is Sekhmet’s husband.",

        "ptah-c02"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Nefertem dianggap sebagai putranya. Ptah menciptakan dunia melalui gagasan dan ucapan. Batu Shabaka menghubungkan pemberian kehidupan dengan hati dan lidahnya. Bentuknya kadang digambarkan sebagai sosok kerdil. Pemujaannya menyebar ke kawasan Mediterania timur. Penggambaran umum memperlihatkan laki-laki berkulit hijau dalam kain pembungkus. Simbol tongkatnya menggabungkan kuasa, kehidupan, dan kestabilan. Sinkretisme menghasilkan Ptah-Sokar-Osiris. World History Encyclopedia menghubungkan Ptah dengan pohon moringa. Dalam peran pencipta, ia juga disebut Ptah-Nun atau Ptah-Naunet. Gambarnya memakai penutup kepala rapat dan memegang tongkat was. Sumber itu menyebut Ptah telah muncul pada Dinasti Pertama. Budge mengaitkan Ptah dengan pembukaan mulut para dewa dalam Kitab Orang Mati.",
      "en": "Nefertem is regarded as his son. Ptah creates the world through conception and speech. The Shabaka Stone connects his giving life with his heart and tongue. He is sometimes represented as a dwarf. His worship spread to the eastern Mediterranean. A common depiction shows a green-skinned man in a shroud. His staff symbols combine power, life and stability. Syncretism produces Ptah-Sokar-Osiris. World History Encyclopedia connects Ptah with the moringa tree. In his creative role he is also called Ptah-Nun or Ptah-Naunet. His image wears a skullcap and holds a was-sceptre. That source states that Ptah appears in the First Dynasty. Budge associates Ptah with opening the gods’ mouths in the Book of the Dead.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ptah-c01",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "is an ancient Egyptian deity, a creator god, and a patron deity of craftsmen and architects.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ptah adalah dewa pencipta Mesir dan pelindung perajin serta arsitek.",
        "en": "Ptah is an Egyptian creator god and patron of craftsmen and architects."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c02",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "In the triad of Memphis, he is the husband of Sekhmet and the father of Nefertem.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam triad Memphis, ia menjadi suami Sekhmet.",
        "en": "In the Memphis triad he is Sekhmet’s husband."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c03",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "In the triad of Memphis, he is the husband of Sekhmet and the father of Nefertem.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nefertem dianggap sebagai putranya.",
        "en": "Nefertem is regarded as his son."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c04",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "Ptah is an Egyptian creator God who conceived the world and brought it into being through the creative power of speech.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ptah menciptakan dunia melalui gagasan dan ucapan.",
        "en": "Ptah creates the world through conception and speech."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c05",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "the Shabaka Stone, from the Twenty-Fifth Dynasty, says Ptah \"gave life to all the gods and their kas as well, through this heart and this tongue.\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Batu Shabaka menghubungkan pemberian kehidupan dengan hati dan lidahnya.",
        "en": "The Shabaka Stone connects his giving life with his heart and tongue."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c06",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "Sometimes represented as a dwarf, naked and deformed, his popularity would continue to grow during the Late Period.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuknya kadang digambarkan sebagai sosok kerdil.",
        "en": "He is sometimes represented as a dwarf."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c07",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "his worship then moved beyond the borders of Egypt and was exported throughout the Eastern Mediterranean.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya menyebar ke kawasan Mediterania timur.",
        "en": "His worship spread to the eastern Mediterranean."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c08",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "Ptah is generally represented in the guise of a man with green skin, contained in a shroud sticking to the skin, wearing the divine beard, and holding a sceptre combining three powerful symbols of ancient Egyptian religion:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran umum memperlihatkan laki-laki berkulit hijau dalam kain pembungkus.",
        "en": "A common depiction shows a green-skinned man in a shroud."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c09",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "These three combined symbols indicate the three creative powers of the god: power (was), life (ankh) and stability (djed).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Simbol tongkatnya menggabungkan kuasa, kehidupan, dan kestabilan.",
        "en": "His staff symbols combine power, life and stability."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c10",
      "source_id": "ptah-s1",
      "quote": "Gradually he formed with Osiris a new deity called Ptah-Sokar-Osiris.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sinkretisme menghasilkan Ptah-Sokar-Osiris.",
        "en": "Syncretism produces Ptah-Sokar-Osiris."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c11",
      "source_id": "ptah-s2",
      "quote": "He was probably an early fertility god and is associated with the moringa tree which, in an early myth, he liked to rest beneath.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 151",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "World History Encyclopedia menghubungkan Ptah dengan pohon moringa.",
        "en": "World History Encyclopedia connects Ptah with the moringa tree."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c12",
      "source_id": "ptah-s2",
      "quote": "He was sometimes known as Ptah-Nun or Ptah-Naunet in his creative aspect, linking him with the primordial substances of the Ogdoad.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 151",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam peran pencipta, ia juga disebut Ptah-Nun atau Ptah-Naunet.",
        "en": "In his creative role he is also called Ptah-Nun or Ptah-Naunet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c13",
      "source_id": "ptah-s2",
      "quote": "He is depicted as a mummified man wearing a skull cap holding the Was scepter of authority with the ankh and djed symbols at the top.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 151",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambarnya memakai penutup kepala rapat dan memegang tongkat was.",
        "en": "His image wears a skullcap and holds a was-sceptre."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c14",
      "source_id": "ptah-s2",
      "quote": "appears in the First Dynastic Period (c. 3150-2613 BCE)",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 151",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber itu menyebut Ptah telah muncul pada Dinasti Pertama.",
        "en": "That source states that Ptah appears in the First Dynasty."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ptah-c15",
      "source_id": "ptah-s3",
      "quote": "From certain allusions in the Book of the Dead he is known to have \"opened the mouth\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 130",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge mengaitkan Ptah dengan pembukaan mulut para dewa dalam Kitab Orang Mati.",
        "en": "Budge associates Ptah with opening the gods’ mouths in the Book of the Dead."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Maat adalah dewi Mesir yang mempersonifikasikan kebenaran, keteraturan, dan keadilan.",
    "en": "Maat is an Egyptian goddess personifying truth, order and justice.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "maat-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Maat adalah dewi Mesir yang mempersonifikasikan kebenaran, keteraturan, dan keadilan. Ia mengatur bintang, musim, dan tindakan manusia serta dewa.",
      "en": "Maat is an Egyptian goddess personifying truth, order and justice. She regulates stars, seasons and the actions of mortals and gods.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "maat-c01",
        "maat-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Isfet menjadi lawan konseptualnya, melambangkan kekacauan dan ketidakadilan. Catatan awal Maat mencakup Teks Piramida Unas. Thoth menjadi pasangan maskulinnya dalam sejumlah kisah. Maat berperan dalam penimbangan hati di Duat. Bulunya menentukan kelayakan jiwa mencapai akhirat yang bahagia. Firaun digambarkan memakai lambangnya untuk menunjukkan penegakan keadilan. Ia digambarkan sebagai perempuan muda, kadang bersayap atau memakai bulu burung unta. Raja mempunyai tugas menjaga Maat tetap hadir dalam tatanan dunia. Maat tetap hadir bersama jiwa di surga Padang Alang-Alang. Sumber ini menerjemahkan kata ma’at sebagai yang lurus. Prinsip Maat mengaitkan tindakan dengan waktu yang tepat. Menurut sumber ini, keharmonisan Maat menjiwai budaya Mesir. Dalam penafsiran Budge atas satu legenda, Maat membantu Khepera sebagai kebijaksanaan penciptaan.",
      "en": "Isfet is her conceptual opposite, representing chaos and injustice. Early records of Maat include the Pyramid Texts of Unas. Thoth is her masculine counterpart in some accounts. Maat plays a role in weighing the heart in the Duat. Her feather measures whether a soul may reach the blessed afterlife. Pharaohs are depicted with her emblems to express their upholding justice. She is depicted as a young woman, sometimes winged or wearing an ostrich feather. Kings have a duty to maintain Maat in the world’s order. Maat remains with the soul in the paradise of the Field of Reeds. This source translates ma’at as that which is straight. The principle of Maat connects actions with their appropriate time. According to this source, Maat’s harmony permeates Egyptian culture. In Budge’s interpretation of one legend, Maat assists Khepera as creative wisdom.",

      "author": null,

      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "maat-c01",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "is the ancient Egyptian concept of truth, balance, law, morality, and justice. ... Maat was also the goddess who personified this concept",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maat adalah dewi Mesir yang mempersonifikasikan kebenaran, keteraturan, dan keadilan.",
        "en": "Maat is an Egyptian goddess personifying truth, order and justice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c02",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "regulated the stars, seasons, and the actions of mortals and the deities who had brought order from chaos at the moment of creation.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengatur bintang, musim, dan tindakan manusia serta dewa.",
        "en": "She regulates stars, seasons and the actions of mortals and gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c03",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "Her ideological opposite was Isfet (Egyptian jzft), meaning injustice, chaos, violence or to do evil.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Isfet menjadi lawan konseptualnya, melambangkan kekacauan dan ketidakadilan.",
        "en": "Isfet is her conceptual opposite, representing chaos and injustice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c04",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest surviving records indicating that Maat is the norm for nature and society, in this world and the next, were recorded during the Old Kingdom of Egypt, the earliest substantial surviving examples being found in the Pyramid Texts of Unas (c. 2375 BCE and 2345 BCE).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan awal Maat mencakup Teks Piramida Unas.",
        "en": "Early records of Maat include the Pyramid Texts of Unas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c05",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "Later, when most goddesses were paired with a male aspect, her masculine counterpart was Thoth, as their attributes are similar. In other accounts, Thoth was paired off with Seshat, goddess of writing and measure, who is a lesser-known deity.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Thoth menjadi pasangan maskulinnya dalam sejumlah kisah.",
        "en": "Thoth is her masculine counterpart in some accounts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c06",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "her primary role in ancient Egyptian religion dealt with the Weighing of the Heart that took place in the Duat.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maat berperan dalam penimbangan hati di Duat.",
        "en": "Maat plays a role in weighing the heart in the Duat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c07",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "Her feather was the measure that determined whether the souls (considered to reside in the heart) of the departed would reach the paradise of the afterlife successfully.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bulunya menentukan kelayakan jiwa mencapai akhirat yang bahagia.",
        "en": "Her feather measures whether a soul may reach the blessed afterlife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c08",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "Pharaohs are often depicted with the emblems of Maat to emphasise their roles in upholding the laws and righteousness. From the Eighteenth Dynasty (c. 1550 – 1295 BC) Maat was described as the daughter of Ra, indicating that pharaohs were believed to rule through her authority.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Firaun digambarkan memakai lambangnya untuk menunjukkan penegakan keadilan.",
        "en": "Pharaohs are depicted with her emblems to express their upholding justice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c09",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "Maat was the goddess of harmony, justice, and truth represented as a young woman. Sometimes she is depicted with wings on each arm or as a woman with an ostrich feather on her head. The meaning of this emblem is uncertain, although the god Shu, who in some myths is Maat's brother, also wears it.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai perempuan muda, kadang bersayap atau memakai bulu burung unta.",
        "en": "She is depicted as a young woman, sometimes winged or wearing an ostrich feather."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c10",
      "source_id": "maat-s1",
      "quote": "Kings inherited the duty to ensure Maat remained in place, and they with Ra are said to \"live on Maat\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raja mempunyai tugas menjaga Maat tetap hadir dalam tatanan dunia.",
        "en": "Kings have a duty to maintain Maat in the world’s order."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c11",
      "source_id": "maat-s2",
      "quote": "continued as a presence in the paradise of the Field of Reeds.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 114",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maat tetap hadir bersama jiwa di surga Padang Alang-Alang.",
        "en": "Maat remains with the soul in the paradise of the Field of Reeds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c12",
      "source_id": "maat-s2",
      "quote": "The word means \"that which is straight\" and the concept of harmony infused every aspect of an Egyptian's life.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 114",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber ini menerjemahkan kata ma’at sebagai yang lurus.",
        "en": "This source translates ma’at as that which is straight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c13",
      "source_id": "maat-s2",
      "quote": "There is a time for every action and aspect of existence within ma'at but all must be recognized and acted upon at appropriate times.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 114",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Prinsip Maat mengaitkan tindakan dengan waktu yang tepat.",
        "en": "The principle of Maat connects actions with their appropriate time."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c14",
      "source_id": "maat-s2",
      "quote": "Ma'at embodied the principle of ma'at (harmony) which was central to the culture of ancient Egypt.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 114",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sumber ini, keharmonisan Maat menjiwai budaya Mesir.",
        "en": "According to this source, Maat’s harmony permeates Egyptian culture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maat-c15",
      "source_id": "maat-s3",
      "quote": "In arranging his thoughts and their visible forms Khepera was assisted by the goddess Maat",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 35",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam penafsiran Budge atas satu legenda, Maat membantu Khepera sebagai kebijaksanaan penciptaan.",
        "en": "In Budge’s interpretation of one legend, Maat assists Khepera as creative wisdom."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    {
      "id": "Nephthys adalah dewi Mesir anggota Ennead Heliopolis. Ia putri Nut dan Geb.",
      "en": "Nephthys is an Egyptian goddess belonging to the Heliopolitan Ennead. She is a daughter of Nut and Geb.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nephthys-c01",
        "nephthys-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Isis adalah saudarinya dan pasangan dalam ritual pemakaman. Ia disebut saudari sekaligus istri Set. Ia dikaitkan dengan perkabungan, malam, dan perlindungan. Namanya lazim diterjemahkan sebagai Nyonya Rumah atau Nyonya Kuil. Bersama Isis ia melambangkan gerbang pylon kuil. Ia dipandang sebagai dewi pelindung yang melambangkan pengalaman kematian. Sebagian mitos menjadikannya ibu Anubis; kisah lain memberi Anubis ibu berbeda. Ia juga berperan sebagai pengasuh firaun. Ia dikenal sebagai Sahabat Orang Mati. Peratap profesional disebut Layang-Layang Nephthys. Lamentations of Isis and Nephthys dipakai dalam festival, ibadah, dan pemakaman. Dalam teks tersebut ia memanggil jiwa Osiris kembali dari kematian. Budge menyebut tempat Nephthys dalam bahtera matahari saat penciptaan dan kaitannya dengan senja.",
      "en": "Isis is her sister and partner in funerary rites. She is described as Set’s sister and wife. She is associated with mourning, night and protection. Her name is usually translated as Lady of the House or Lady of the Temple. With Isis she symbolizes a temple’s pylon gateway. She is viewed as a protective goddess symbolizing the death experience. Some myths make her Anubis’s mother; others give him a different mother. She also acts as a nurse to the pharaoh. She is known as Friend of the Dead. Professional mourners are called the Kites of Nephthys. The Lamentations of Isis and Nephthys is used at festivals, services and funerals. In that text she calls Osiris’s soul back from death. Budge describes Nephthys’s place in the solar boat at creation and her connection with twilight.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c01",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "was a goddess in ancient Egyptian religion. A member of the Great Ennead of Heliopolis in Egyptian mythology",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nephthys adalah dewi Mesir anggota Ennead Heliopolis.",
        "en": "Nephthys is an Egyptian goddess belonging to the Heliopolitan Ennead."

    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c02",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "she was a daughter of Nut and Geb.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia putri Nut dan Geb.",
        "en": "She is a daughter of Nut and Geb."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c03",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "Nephthys was typically paired with her sister Isis in funerary rites because of their role as protectresses of the mummy",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Isis adalah saudarinya dan pasangan dalam ritual pemakaman.",
        "en": "Isis is her sister and partner in funerary rites."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c04",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "as the sister-wife of Set. She",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut saudari sekaligus istri Set.",
        "en": "She is described as Set’s sister and wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c05",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "She was associated with mourning, the night/darkness, service (specifically temples), childbirth, the dead, protection, magic, health, embalming, and beer.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikaitkan dengan perkabungan, malam, dan perlindungan.",
        "en": "She is associated with mourning, night and protection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c06",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "Nephthys is the Greek form of an epithet (transliterated as Nebet-hut, Nebet-het, Nebt-het, from Egyptian nbt-ḥwt). The origin of the goddess Nephthys is unclear but the literal translation of her name is usually given as Lady of the House or Lady of the Temple.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya lazim diterjemahkan sebagai Nyonya Rumah atau Nyonya Kuil.",
        "en": "Her name is usually translated as Lady of the House or Lady of the Temple."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c07",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "Along with her sister Isis, Nephthys represented the temple pylon or trapezoidal tower gateway entrance to the temple",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bersama Isis ia melambangkan gerbang pylon kuil.",
        "en": "With Isis she symbolizes a temple’s pylon gateway."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c08",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "Nephthys is a protective goddess who symbolizes the death experience",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipandang sebagai dewi pelindung yang melambangkan pengalaman kematian.",
        "en": "She is viewed as a protective goddess symbolizing the death experience."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c09",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "Nephthys is regarded as the mother of the funerary deity Anubis (Inpu) in some myths. Alternatively Anubis appears as the son of Bastet or Isis. In Nubia, Nephthys was said to be the wife of Anubis.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian mitos menjadikannya ibu Anubis; kisah lain memberi Anubis ibu berbeda.",
        "en": "Some myths make her Anubis’s mother; others give him a different mother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c10",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s1",
      "quote": "Nephthys also was considered to be the nurse of the reigning pharaoh himself.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga berperan sebagai pengasuh firaun.",
        "en": "She also acts as a nurse to the pharaoh."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c11",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s2",
      "quote": "She was known as \"Friend of the Dead\" for her care of the souls in the afterlife",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 139",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikenal sebagai Sahabat Orang Mati.",
        "en": "She is known as Friend of the Dead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c12",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s2",
      "quote": "professional mourners at funerals, who encouraged the open expression of grief, were known as the \"Kites of Nephthys\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 139",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peratap profesional disebut Layang-Layang Nephthys.",
        "en": "Professional mourners are called the Kites of Nephthys."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c13",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s2",
      "quote": "This text was recited regularly at festivals, services, and funerals throughout Egypt.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 139",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lamentations of Isis and Nephthys dipakai dalam festival, ibadah, dan pemakaman.",
        "en": "The Lamentations of Isis and Nephthys is used at festivals, services and funerals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c14",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s2",
      "quote": "In the text The Lamentations of Isis and Nephthys she calls the soul of Osiris back from the dead.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 139",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam teks tersebut ia memanggil jiwa Osiris kembali dari kematian.",
        "en": "In that text she calls Osiris’s soul back from death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nephthys-c15",
      "source_id": "nephthys-s3",
      "quote": "like her she had a place in the boat of the Sun at creation, when she probably typified the twilight or very early night.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 127",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge menyebut tempat Nephthys dalam bahtera matahari saat penciptaan dan kaitannya dengan senja.",
        "en": "Budge describes Nephthys’s place in the solar boat at creation and her connection with twilight."
      }

    "en": "Nut is the goddess of sky and stars in ancient Egyptian religion.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nut adalah dewi langit dan bintang dalam agama Mesir kuno. Ia sering digambarkan sebagai perempuan berbintang yang melengkung di atas bumi.",
      "en": "Nut is the goddess of sky and stars in ancient Egyptian religion. She is often depicted as a star-covered woman arching over the earth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nut-c01",
        "nut-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia juga dapat digambarkan sebagai sapi. Namanya juga berarti langit dan memiliki sejumlah transkripsi. Shu dan Tefnut adalah orang tuanya. Geb adalah saudara laki-laki sekaligus suaminya. Anak-anaknya mencakup Osiris, Set, Isis, dan Nephthys. Asalnya berkaitan dengan kisah penciptaan Heliopolis. Tangga maqet menjadi lambang Nut dan dipasang di makam untuk perlindungan. Nut mewakili langit, sedangkan Geb mewakili bumi. Salah satu kisah melarang Nut melahirkan pada hari mana pun dalam tahun semula. Thoth memenangkan cahaya bulan senilai lima hari dari Iah. Hari tambahan memungkinkan Nut melahirkan kelima anaknya. Versi lain menjadikan Khonsu sebagai pihak yang kalah dalam permainan itu. Budge mencatat Nut dan Geb sebagai pemberi makanan bagi orang hidup maupun mati.",
      "en": "She can also be depicted as a cow. Her name also means sky and has several transcriptions. Shu and Tefnut are her parents. Geb is her brother and husband. Her children include Osiris, Set, Isis and Nephthys. Her origins are linked to the Heliopolitan creation story. The maqet ladder is a symbol of Nut placed in tombs for protection. Nut personifies the sky while Geb personifies the earth. One tale forbids Nut to give birth on any day of the original year. Thoth wins five days’ worth of moonlight from Iah. The additional days allow Nut to bear her five children. Another version makes Khonsu the loser in that game. Budge records Nut and Geb as givers of food to both the living and dead.",

      "quote": "She is often depicted as a nude woman covered with stars and arching over the Earth; and sometimes as a cow. Alternatively, she is identified with a water-pot (nw) above her head.",

    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c07",
      "source_id": "nut-s1",
      "quote": "She had four children – Osiris, Set, Isis, and Nephthys",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anak-anaknya mencakup Osiris, Set, Isis, dan Nephthys.",
        "en": "Her children include Osiris, Set, Isis and Nephthys."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c08",
      "source_id": "nut-s1",
      "quote": "her origin being found on the creation story of Heliopolis.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asalnya berkaitan dengan kisah penciptaan Heliopolis.",
        "en": "Her origins are linked to the Heliopolitan creation story."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c09",
      "source_id": "nut-s1",
      "quote": "A sacred symbol of Nut was the ladder used by Osiris to enter her heavenly skies. This ladder-symbol was called maqet and was placed in tombs to protect the deceased, and to invoke the aid of the deity of the dead. Nut and her brother, Geb, may be considered enigmas in the world of mythology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tangga maqet menjadi lambang Nut dan dipasang di makam untuk perlindungan.",
        "en": "The maqet ladder is a symbol of Nut placed in tombs for protection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c10",
      "source_id": "nut-s1",
      "quote": "she personified the sky and he the Earth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nut mewakili langit, sedangkan Geb mewakili bumi.",
        "en": "Nut personifies the sky while Geb personifies the earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c11",
      "source_id": "nut-s2",
      "quote": "He also decreed that she could not give birth on any day of the year.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 141",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu kisah melarang Nut melahirkan pada hari mana pun dalam tahun semula.",
        "en": "One tale forbids Nut to give birth on any day of the original year."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c12",
      "source_id": "nut-s2",
      "quote": "Thoth, the god of wisdom, gambled with Iah, god of the moon, and won five days worth of moonlight which he transformed into days.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 141",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Thoth memenangkan cahaya bulan senilai lima hari dari Iah.",
        "en": "Thoth wins five days’ worth of moonlight from Iah."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c13",
      "source_id": "nut-s2",
      "quote": "Nut was able to then give birth to her five children on five consecutive days in July",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 141",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hari tambahan memungkinkan Nut melahirkan kelima anaknya.",
        "en": "The additional days allow Nut to bear her five children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c14",
      "source_id": "nut-s2",
      "quote": "In some versions of the story it is Khonsu who loses the gamble with Thoth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 141",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain menjadikan Khonsu sebagai pihak yang kalah dalam permainan itu.",
        "en": "Another version makes Khonsu the loser in that game."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nut-c15",
      "source_id": "nut-s3",
      "quote": "she and her husband Seb were considered to be the givers of food, not only to the living but also to the dead.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 123",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge mencatat Nut dan Geb sebagai pemberi makanan bagi orang hidup maupun mati.",
        "en": "Budge records Nut and Geb as givers of food to both the living and dead."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {

    "en": "Atum is an Egyptian primordial god from whom everything arises.",

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Atum adalah dewa primordial Mesir yang menjadi asal segala sesuatu. Ia menciptakan dirinya sendiri.",
      "en": "Atum is an Egyptian primordial god from whom everything arises. He creates himself.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "atum-c01",
        "atum-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia adalah ayah Shu dan Tefnut. Atum erat terkait matahari senja. Namanya dikaitkan dengan kata kerja yang berarti melengkapi atau menyelesaikan. Teks Piramida memadukannya dengan Ra sebagai Ra-Atum. Teologi Memphis mengisahkan Ptah menciptakan Atum melalui pikiran dan ucapan. Dalam kosmogoni Heliopolis ia muncul dari air primordial pada benben. Ia dipercaya mengangkat jiwa raja dari piramida ke langit berbintang. Teks Peti Mati menggambarkan Atum dan Osiris bertahan sebagai ular pada akhir alam semesta. Sumber ini menempatkan Atum sebagai dewa pertama Ennead. Ia memakai daya magis Heka dalam penciptaan. Ia juga disebut pencipta umat manusia. Budge membedakan Atum sebagai penutup hari dari Ptah sebagai pembuka hari. Budge menyatakan bentuk manusia Atum dikenal pada masa raja Dinasti Kelima.",
      "en": "He is the father of Shu and Tefnut. Atum is closely associated with the evening sun. His name is connected with a verb meaning to complete or finish. The Pyramid Texts combine him with Ra as Ra-Atum. Memphite theology tells of Ptah creating Atum through thought and speech. In Heliopolitan cosmogony he emerges from primordial waters on the benben mound. He is believed to lift a king’s soul from his pyramid to the starry heavens. The Coffin Texts depict Atum and Osiris surviving as serpents at the universe’s end. This source places Atum as the first god of the Ennead. He uses Heka’s magical power in creation. He is also described as creator of humankind. Budge distinguishes Atum as closer of the day from Ptah as opener of the day. Budge states that Atum’s human form was known under Fifth-Dynasty kings.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "atum-c03",
        "atum-c04",

        "id": "Atum adalah dewa primordial Mesir yang menjadi asal segala sesuatu.",
        "en": "Atum is an Egyptian primordial god from whom everything arises."

    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c02",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "He created himself and is the father of Shu and Tefnut",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menciptakan dirinya sendiri.",
        "en": "He creates himself."

    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c03",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "He created himself and is the father of Shu and Tefnut",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "He is the father of Shu and Tefnut."

    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c04",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "Atum is also closely associated with the evening sun.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Atum erat terkait matahari senja.",
        "en": "Atum is closely associated with the evening sun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c05",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "Atum's name is thought to be derived from the verb tm which means 'to complete' or 'to finish'. Thus, he has been interpreted as being the \"complete one\" and also the finisher of the world, which he returns to watery chaos at the end of the creative cycle. As creator, he was seen as the progenitor of the world, the deities and universe having received his vital force or ka.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dikaitkan dengan kata kerja yang berarti melengkapi atau menyelesaikan.",
        "en": "His name is connected with a verb meaning to complete or finish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c06",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "he is sometimes syncretized with Ra to form Ra-Atum",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Piramida memadukannya dengan Ra sebagai Ra-Atum.",
        "en": "The Pyramid Texts combine him with Ra as Ra-Atum."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c07",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "The Memphites (priests of Memphis), on the other hand, believed that Ptah created Atum in a more intellectual way, using his speech and thought, as told on the Shabaka Stone.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teologi Memphis mengisahkan Ptah menciptakan Atum melalui pikiran dan ucapan.",
        "en": "Memphite theology tells of Ptah creating Atum through thought and speech."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c08",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "In the Heliopolitan Cosmogony, Atum was considered to be the first god, having created himself, sitting on a mound (benben) (or identified with the mound itself), and rose from the primordial waters (Nu). Early myths state that Atum created the god Shu and goddess Tefnut by spitting them out of his mouth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kosmogoni Heliopolis ia muncul dari air primordial pada benben.",
        "en": "In Heliopolitan cosmogony he emerges from primordial waters on the benben mound."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c09",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "In the Old Kingdom, the Egyptians believed that Atum lifted the dead king's soul from his pyramid to the starry heavens. He was also a solar deity, associated with the primary sun god Ra. Atum was linked specifically with the evening sun, while Ra or the closely linked god Khepri were connected with the sun at morning and midday.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipercaya mengangkat jiwa raja dari piramida ke langit berbintang.",
        "en": "He is believed to lift a king’s soul from his pyramid to the starry heavens."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c10",
      "source_id": "atum-s1",
      "quote": "after millions of years he and Osiris would be the only ones to survive the end of time as serpents.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Peti Mati menggambarkan Atum dan Osiris bertahan sebagai ular pada akhir alam semesta.",
        "en": "The Coffin Texts depict Atum and Osiris surviving as serpents at the universe’s end."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c11",
      "source_id": "atum-s2",
      "quote": "first god of the Ennead (tribunal of nine gods)",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 41",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber ini menempatkan Atum sebagai dewa pertama Ennead.",
        "en": "This source places Atum as the first god of the Ennead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c12",
      "source_id": "atum-s2",
      "quote": "draws on the magical forces of Heka to create all the other gods, human beings, and life on earth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 41",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memakai daya magis Heka dalam penciptaan.",
        "en": "He uses Heka’s magical power in creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atum-c13",
      "source_id": "atum-s2",
      "quote": "creator of the universe and human beings.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 41",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {

        "en": "He is also described as creator of humankind."

    },
    {

      "quote": "TEMU or ATMU, i.e., the \"closer\" of the day, just as Ptah was the \"opener\" of the day.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 119",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

    },

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mut adalah dewi ibu yang dipuja di Mesir kuno. Namanya berarti ibu.",
      "en": "Mut is a mother goddess worshipped in ancient Egypt. Her name means mother.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mut-c01",
        "mut-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia berkaitan dengan air primordial Nu. Sebagian kisah menyebutnya melahirkan dunia tanpa pasangan. Amun-Ra lebih sering disebut sebagai suaminya. Khonsu adalah anak bulan yang terkait dengannya. Mut, Amun-Ra, dan Khonsu membentuk triad Thebes di Karnak. Seni menggambarkannya memakai mahkota ganda Mesir. Pemujaannya didukung penguasa untuk menegaskan kewenangan mereka. Ia terlibat dalam Festival Opet dan Festival Lembah. Mantra 164 Kitab Orang Mati menggambarkan Mut menyelamatkan jiwa dari setan. Ia adalah pelindung ilahi raja dan negara. Sumber ini menyebutnya membakar pengkhianat dalam anglo api. Ia dikaitkan dengan Bastet dan Sekhmet sebagai dewi pelindung. Mackenzie mencatat bentuk Mut sebagai burung nasar dan singa betina.",
      "en": "She is connected with Nu’s primordial waters. Some accounts describe her giving birth to the world without a partner. Amun-Ra is more often named as her husband. Khonsu is the lunar child associated with her. Mut, Amun-Ra and Khonsu form the Theban triad at Karnak. Art depicts her wearing Egypt’s double crown. Rulers supported her worship to assert their authority. She participates in the Opet Festival and Beautiful Festival of the Valley. Spell 164 of the Book of the Dead portrays Mut saving souls from demons. She is a divine protector of the king and state. This source describes her burning traitors in a flaming brazier. She is linked with Bastet and Sekhmet as a protective goddess. Mackenzie records Mut’s forms as a vulture and a lioness.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "mut-c05",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berkaitan dengan air primordial Nu.",
        "en": "She is connected with Nu’s primordial waters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c04",
      "source_id": "mut-s1",
      "quote": "Mut was sometimes said to have given birth to the world through parthenogenesis",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kisah menyebutnya melahirkan dunia tanpa pasangan.",
        "en": "Some accounts describe her giving birth to the world without a partner."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c05",
      "source_id": "mut-s1",
      "quote": "more often she was said to have a husband, the solar creator god Amun-Ra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Amun-Ra lebih sering disebut sebagai suaminya.",
        "en": "Amun-Ra is more often named as her husband."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c06",
      "source_id": "mut-s1",
      "quote": "she was particularly associated as the mother of the lunar child god Khonsu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khonsu adalah anak bulan yang terkait dengannya.",
        "en": "Khonsu is the lunar child associated with her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c07",
      "source_id": "mut-s1",
      "quote": "the family of Amun-Ra, Mut and Khonsu were worshipped together as the Theban Triad.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mut, Amun-Ra, dan Khonsu membentuk triad Thebes di Karnak.",
        "en": "Mut, Amun-Ra and Khonsu form the Theban triad at Karnak."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c08",
      "source_id": "mut-s1",
      "quote": "In art, Mut was usually depicted as a woman wearing the double crown of the kings of Egypt, representing her power over the whole of the land.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seni menggambarkannya memakai mahkota ganda Mesir.",
        "en": "Art depicts her wearing Egypt’s double crown."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c09",
      "source_id": "mut-s1",
      "quote": "During the high point of Mut's cult, the rulers of Egypt would support her worship in their own way to emphasize their own authority and right to rule through an association with Mut. Mut was involved in many ancient Egyptian festivals such as the Opet Festival and the Beautiful Festival of the Valley.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya didukung penguasa untuk menegaskan kewenangan mereka.",
        "en": "Rulers supported her worship to assert their authority."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c10",
      "source_id": "mut-s1",
      "quote": "Mut was involved in many ancient Egyptian festivals such as the Opet Festival and the Beautiful Festival of the Valley.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia terlibat dalam Festival Opet dan Festival Lembah.",
        "en": "She participates in the Opet Festival and Beautiful Festival of the Valley."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c11",
      "source_id": "mut-s2",
      "quote": "in Spell 164 of the Book of the Dead, is depicted as a savior of souls trapped by demons in the afterlife.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 130",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mantra 164 Kitab Orang Mati menggambarkan Mut menyelamatkan jiwa dari setan.",
        "en": "Spell 164 of the Book of the Dead portrays Mut saving souls from demons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c12",
      "source_id": "mut-s2",
      "quote": "She was also the divine protector of the king and state",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 130",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah pelindung ilahi raja dan negara.",
        "en": "She is a divine protector of the king and state."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c13",
      "source_id": "mut-s2",
      "quote": "roasted conspirators and traitors in her flaming brazier.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 130",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber ini menyebutnya membakar pengkhianat dalam anglo api.",
        "en": "This source describes her burning traitors in a flaming brazier."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c14",
      "source_id": "mut-s2",
      "quote": "Mut was a protector deity associated with Bastet and Sekhmet.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 130",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikaitkan dengan Bastet dan Sekhmet sebagai dewi pelindung.",
        "en": "She is linked with Bastet and Sekhmet as a protective goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mut-c15",
      "source_id": "mut-s3",
      "quote": "She is represented as a vulture and also as a lioness.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 15",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie mencatat bentuk Mut sebagai burung nasar dan singa betina.",
        "en": "Mackenzie records Mut’s forms as a vulture and a lioness."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "id": "Sekhmet adalah dewi prajurit dan penyembuhan dalam mitologi Mesir.",
    "en": "Sekhmet is an Egyptian warrior and healing goddess.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sekhmet adalah dewi prajurit dan penyembuhan dalam mitologi Mesir. Sekhmet juga merupakan dewi matahari dengan gelar Mata Ra.",
      "en": "Sekhmet is an Egyptian warrior and healing goddess. Sekhmet is also a solar deity with the epithet Eye of Ra.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sekhmet-c01",
        "sekhmet-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia sering dikaitkan dengan Hathor dan Bastet. Sekhmet adalah manifestasi pembalasan Ra yang disebut Mata Ra. Ia dianggap menyebabkan wabah, sekaligus dimohon untuk menyembuhkan. Ra mengirim Hathor dalam bentuk Sekhmet menghukum manusia yang bersekongkol. Para dewa menghentikannya dengan bir merah yang disangka darah. Versi lain mengisahkan Thoth membujuk Sekhmet kembali setelah ia meninggalkan Mesir. Ia adalah istri Ptah dan ibu Nefertum. Festival mabuk mengulang minum yang meredakan amarahnya dalam mitos. Namanya diartikan Yang Perkasa. Ia menjadi pelindung militer melalui kekuatan penghancuran dan pembalasan. Salah satu gelarnya adalah Penghantam Orang Nubia. Gelar Nyonya Kehidupan berkaitan dengan kemampuannya menyembuhkan wabah. Budge memakai nama Sekhet untuk pasangan feminin Ptah dalam triad bersama Nefertem.",
      "en": "She is often associated with Hathor and Bastet. Sekhmet is a vengeful manifestation of Ra called the Eye of Ra. She is believed to cause plagues and is also invoked for healing. Ra sends Hathor as Sekhmet to punish conspiring mortals. The gods stop her with red beer mistaken for blood. Another version tells of Thoth persuading Sekhmet to return after she leaves Egypt. She is Ptah’s wife and Nefertum’s mother. A festival of intoxication reenacts the drinking that calms her mythical wrath. Her name is interpreted as the Powerful One. She becomes a military patron through destruction and vengeance. One of her titles is Smiter of the Nubians. The Mistress of Life title relates to her ability to cure plague. Budge uses Sekhet for Ptah’s feminine counterpart in a triad with Nefertem.",

    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c03",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s1",
      "quote": "She is often associated with the goddesses Hathor and Bastet.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia sering dikaitkan dengan Hathor dan Bastet.",
        "en": "She is often associated with Hathor and Bastet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c04",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s1",
      "quote": "Sekhmet acted as the vengeful manifestation of Ra's power, the Eye of Ra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekhmet adalah manifestasi pembalasan Ra yang disebut Mata Ra.",
        "en": "Sekhmet is a vengeful manifestation of Ra called the Eye of Ra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c05",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s1",
      "quote": "She is also believed to cause plagues ... she is also called upon to ward off disease and heal the sick.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap menyebabkan wabah, sekaligus dimohon untuk menyembuhkan.",
        "en": "She is believed to cause plagues and is also invoked for healing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c06",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s1",
      "quote": "Ra sends the goddess Hathor, in the form of Sekhmet, to destroy mortals who conspired against him.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ra mengirim Hathor dalam bentuk Sekhmet menghukum manusia yang bersekongkol.",
        "en": "Ra sends Hathor as Sekhmet to punish conspiring mortals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c07",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s1",
      "quote": "They poured out a lake of beer dyed with red ochre so that it resembled blood. Mistaking the beer for blood, Sekhmet drank it all and became so drunk that she gave up on the slaughter and returned peacefully to Ra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para dewa menghentikannya dengan bir merah yang disangka darah.",
        "en": "The gods stop her with red beer mistaken for blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c08",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s1",
      "quote": "In other versions of this story, Sekhmet grew angered at the deception and left Egypt, diminishing the power of the sun. This threatened the power and security of the world; thus, she was persuaded by the god Thoth to return and restore the sun to its full glory.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain mengisahkan Thoth membujuk Sekhmet kembali setelah ia meninggalkan Mesir.",
        "en": "Another version tells of Thoth persuading Sekhmet to return after she leaves Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c09",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s1",
      "quote": "Sekhmet was considered the wife of the god Ptah and mother of his son Nefertum.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah istri Ptah dan ibu Nefertum.",
        "en": "She is Ptah’s wife and Nefertum’s mother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c10",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s1",
      "quote": "During an annual festival held at the beginning of the year, a festival of intoxication, the Egyptians danced and played music to soothe the wildness of the goddess and drank great quantities of beer and wine ritually to imitate the extreme drunkenness that stopped the wrath of the goddess—when she almost destroyed humanity.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Festival mabuk mengulang minum yang meredakan amarahnya dalam mitos.",
        "en": "A festival of intoxication reenacts the drinking that calms her mythical wrath."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c11",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s2",
      "quote": "Her name means \"Powerful\" and is usually interpreted as \"The Female Powerful One\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 170",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya diartikan Yang Perkasa.",
        "en": "Her name is interpreted as the Powerful One."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c12",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s2",
      "quote": "was the patron deity of the military for her powers of destruction and vengeance.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 170",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi pelindung militer melalui kekuatan penghancuran dan pembalasan.",
        "en": "She becomes a military patron through destruction and vengeance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c13",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s2",
      "quote": "She was known as \"Smiter of the Nubians\" in this regard but she also brought natural disaster.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 170",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu gelarnya adalah Penghantam Orang Nubia.",
        "en": "One of her titles is Smiter of the Nubians."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c14",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s2",
      "quote": "she could cure it and was known as \"Mistress of Life\" in this capacity",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 170",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gelar Nyonya Kehidupan berkaitan dengan kemampuannya menyembuhkan wabah.",
        "en": "The Mistress of Life title relates to her ability to cure plague."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sekhmet-c15",
      "source_id": "sekhmet-s3",
      "quote": "His feminine counterpart was the goddess SEKHET, and the third member of the triad of which he was the chief was NEFER-TEMU.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 130",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge memakai nama Sekhet untuk pasangan feminin Ptah dalam triad bersama Nefertem.",
        "en": "Budge uses Sekhet for Ptah’s feminine counterpart in a triad with Nefertem."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Geb is the Egyptian earth god and a member of the Heliopolitan Ennead.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Geb adalah dewa bumi Mesir dan anggota Ennead Heliopolis. Tawanya dipercaya menghasilkan gempa bumi.",
      "en": "Geb is the Egyptian earth god and a member of the Heliopolitan Ennead. His laughter is believed to produce earthquakes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "geb-c01",
        "geb-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia juga memungkinkan tanaman tumbuh. Penggambaran tertua yang disebut artikel berasal dari masa Djoser. Ia kadang digambarkan sebagai domba, banteng, atau buaya. Teks Peti Mati menyebutnya ayah ular mitologis Nehebkau. Nut adalah istrinya, sedangkan Shu dan Tefnut adalah orang tuanya. Anak-anaknya mencakup Osiris, Set, Isis, dan Nephthys. Shu memisahkan Geb dari Nut. Penggambarannya dapat memuat tanaman pada tubuhnya. World History Encyclopedia menegaskan Geb sebagai dewa bumi dan tumbuhan yang tumbuh. Budge mencatat gelar kepala turun-temurun para dewa bagi Geb. Menurut Budge, peran Geb berkembang menjadi dewa orang mati karena mereka dimakamkan di bumi. Budge menghubungkan Geb dan Nut dengan pemberian makanan bagi orang mati. Budge juga mencatat gelar ayah para dewa untuk Geb.",
      "en": "He also enables crops to grow. The earliest representation cited by the article dates to Djoser’s reign. He is sometimes depicted as a ram, bull or crocodile. The Coffin Texts call him father of the mythical serpent Nehebkau. Nut is his wife and Shu and Tefnut are his parents. His children include Osiris, Set, Isis and Nephthys. Shu separates Geb from Nut. His representations may show plants on his body. World History Encyclopedia identifies Geb with earth and growing plants. Budge records the title hereditary chief of the gods for Geb. According to Budge, Geb’s role develops into a god of the dead because they are buried in the earth. Budge connects Geb and Nut with providing food for the dead. Budge also records the title father of the gods for Geb.",

      "url": "https://www.worldhistory.org/article/885/egyptian-gods---the-complete-list/",
      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-s3",
      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/11277/pg11277-images.html",
      "title": "Egyptian Ideas of the Future Life",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Project Gutenberg",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "geb-c01",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "was the Egyptian god of the Earth and a mythological member of the Ennead of Heliopolis.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Geb adalah dewa bumi Mesir dan anggota Ennead Heliopolis.",
        "en": "Geb is the Egyptian earth god and a member of the Heliopolitan Ennead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c02",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "It was believed in ancient Egypt that Geb's laughter generated earthquakes and that he allowed crops to grow.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tawanya dipercaya menghasilkan gempa bumi.",
        "en": "His laughter is believed to produce earthquakes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c03",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "It was believed in ancient Egypt that Geb's laughter generated earthquakes and that he allowed crops to grow.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga memungkinkan tanaman tumbuh.",
        "en": "He also enables crops to grow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c04",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "The oldest representation in a fragmentary relief of the god was as an anthropomorphic bearded being accompanied by his name, and dating from king Djoser's reign, during the Third Dynasty, and was found in Heliopolis. However, the god never received a temple of his own.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran tertua yang disebut artikel berasal dari masa Djoser.",
        "en": "The earliest representation cited by the article dates to Djoser’s reign."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c05",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "In later times he could also be depicted as a ram, a bull or a crocodile",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia kadang digambarkan sebagai domba, banteng, atau buaya.",
        "en": "He is sometimes depicted as a ram, bull or crocodile."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c06",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "In one of the Coffin Text spells Geb was described as father of the mythological snake Nehebkau of primeval times.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Peti Mati menyebutnya ayah ular mitologis Nehebkau.",
        "en": "The Coffin Texts call him father of the mythical serpent Nehebkau."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c07",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "In the Heliopolitan Ennead (a group of nine gods created in the beginning by the one god Atum or Ra), Geb is the husband of Nut, the sky or visible daytime and nightly firmament, the son of the earlier primordial elements Tefnut (moisture) and Shu (\"emptiness\"), and the father to the four lesser gods of the system – Osiris, Seth, Isis and Nephthys.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nut adalah istrinya, sedangkan Shu dan Tefnut adalah orang tuanya.",
        "en": "Nut is his wife and Shu and Tefnut are his parents."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c08",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "the father to the four lesser gods of the system – Osiris, Seth, Isis and Nephthys.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anak-anaknya mencakup Osiris, Set, Isis, dan Nephthys.",
        "en": "His children include Osiris, Set, Isis and Nephthys."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c09",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "Geb was believed to have originally been engaged with Nut and had to be separated from her by Shu, god of the air.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shu memisahkan Geb dari Nut.",
        "en": "Shu separates Geb from Nut."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c10",
      "source_id": "geb-s1",
      "quote": "was depicted with plants and other green patches on his body.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambarannya dapat memuat tanaman pada tubuhnya.",
        "en": "His representations may show plants on his body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c11",
      "source_id": "geb-s2",
      "quote": "Geb - God of the earth and growing things.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 65",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "World History Encyclopedia menegaskan Geb sebagai dewa bumi dan tumbuhan yang tumbuh.",
        "en": "World History Encyclopedia identifies Geb with earth and growing plants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c12",
      "source_id": "geb-s3",
      "quote": "He is called the \"Erpā,\" i.e., the \"hereditary chief\" of the gods",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 122",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge mencatat gelar kepala turun-temurun para dewa bagi Geb.",
        "en": "Budge records the title hereditary chief of the gods for Geb."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c13",
      "source_id": "geb-s3",
      "quote": "later he became a god of the dead as representing the earth wherein the deceased was laid.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 122",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Budge, peran Geb berkembang menjadi dewa orang mati karena mereka dimakamkan di bumi.",
        "en": "According to Budge, Geb’s role develops into a god of the dead because they are buried in the earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c14",
      "source_id": "geb-s3",
      "quote": "she and her husband Seb were considered to be the givers of food, not only to the living but also to the dead.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 123",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge menghubungkan Geb dan Nut dengan pemberian makanan bagi orang mati.",
        "en": "Budge connects Geb and Nut with providing food for the dead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geb-c15",
      "source_id": "geb-s3",
      "quote": "the \"father of the gods,\" these being, of course, Osiris, Isis, Set, and Nephthys.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 122",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge juga mencatat gelar ayah para dewa untuk Geb.",
        "en": "Budge also records the title father of the gods for Geb."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    {
      "id": "Shu adalah dewa udara dan angin Mesir, anggota Ennead Heliopolis. Tefnut adalah saudari sekaligus istrinya.",
      "en": "Shu is an Egyptian god of air and wind belonging to the Heliopolitan Ennead. Tefnut is his sister and wife.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shu-c01",
        "shu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Atum menciptakan Shu dan Tefnut dalam kisah Heliopolis. Shu adalah ayah Nut dan Geb. Udara yang diwakilinya dianggap menyejukkan dan menenangkan. Ia digambarkan memakai bulu burung unta sebagai lambang ringan dan kosong. Kabut dan awan juga dikaitkan dengan Shu. Sebuah mitos mengisahkan Tefnut meninggalkan Mesir setelah bertengkar dengan Shu. Orang Yunani menghubungkannya dengan Atlas yang menopang langit. Ia umumnya berupa manusia, kadang berkepala singa saat membela dewa matahari. Nama Shu diterjemahkan sebagai Kekosongan. Satu kisah mengisahkan Mata Ra mencari Shu dan Tefnut yang lama pergi. Kepulangan mereka membuat Atum menangis bahagia dan air matanya menjadi manusia. Kabut disebut Danau Shu dalam uraian sumber ini. Budge menjelaskan empat pilar di arah mata angin sebagai penopang langit Shu.",
      "en": "Atum creates Shu and Tefnut in the Heliopolitan account. Shu is the father of Nut and Geb. The air he represents is considered cooling and calming. He is shown wearing an ostrich feather signifying lightness and emptiness. Mist and clouds are also associated with Shu. One myth tells of Tefnut leaving Egypt after a quarrel with Shu. The Greeks associate him with Atlas who supports the heavens. He is usually human, sometimes lion-headed when defending the sun god. Shu’s name is translated as Emptiness. One tale has the Eye of Ra search for Shu and Tefnut after a long absence. Their return makes Atum weep for joy and his tears become humans. Mist is called Lakes of Shu in this source. Budge describes four pillars at the cardinal points as Shu’s sky supports.",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "shu-c01",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "one of the nine deities of the Ennead of the Heliopolis cosmogony. He was the god of light, peace, lions, air, and wind.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shu adalah dewa udara dan angin Mesir, anggota Ennead Heliopolis.",
        "en": "Shu is an Egyptian god of air and wind belonging to the Heliopolitan Ennead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c02",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "spouse and brother to the goddess Tefnut",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tefnut adalah saudari sekaligus istrinya.",
        "en": "Tefnut is his sister and wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c03",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "In Heliopolitan theology, Atum created the first couple of the Ennead, Shu and Tefnut, by masturbating or by spitting. Shu was the father of Nut and Geb and grandfather of Osiris, Isis, Set, and Nephthys. His great-grandsons are Horus and Anubis.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Atum menciptakan Shu dan Tefnut dalam kisah Heliopolis.",
        "en": "Atum creates Shu and Tefnut in the Heliopolitan account."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c04",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "Shu was the father of Nut and Geb",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shu adalah ayah Nut dan Geb.",
        "en": "Shu is the father of Nut and Geb."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c05",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "As the air, Shu was considered to be a cooling, and thus a calming influence and pacifier. Due to the association with dry air, calm, and thus Ma'at (truth, justice, order, and balance), Shu was depicted as the dry air/atmosphere between the Earth and sky, separating the two realms after the event of the First Occasion. Shu was also portrayed in art as wearing an ostrich feather.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Udara yang diwakilinya dianggap menyejukkan dan menenangkan.",
        "en": "The air he represents is considered cooling and calming."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c06",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "Shu was also portrayed in art as wearing an ostrich feather. ... The ostrich feather was symbolic of lightness and emptiness.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan memakai bulu burung unta sebagai lambang ringan dan kosong.",
        "en": "He is shown wearing an ostrich feather signifying lightness and emptiness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c07",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "Fog and clouds were also Shu's elements and they were often called his bones.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kabut dan awan juga dikaitkan dengan Shu.",
        "en": "Mist and clouds are also associated with Shu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c08",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "In a much later myth, representing a terrible weather disaster at the end of the Old Kingdom, it's said that Tefnut and Shu once argued, and Tefnut left Egypt for Nubia (which was always more temperate). It was said that Shu quickly decided that he missed her, but she changed into a cat that destroyed any man or god that approached.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah mitos mengisahkan Tefnut meninggalkan Mesir setelah bertengkar dengan Shu.",
        "en": "One myth tells of Tefnut leaving Egypt after a quarrel with Shu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c09",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "The Greeks associated Shu with Atlas, the primordial Titan who held up the celestial spheres, as they are both depicted holding up the sky.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Yunani menghubungkannya dengan Atlas yang menopang langit.",
        "en": "The Greeks associate him with Atlas who supports the heavens."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c10",
      "source_id": "shu-s1",
      "quote": "Shu is mostly represented as a human. Only in his function as a fighter and defender of the sun god is he sometimes depicted with a lion's head. He carries an ankh, the symbol of life.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia umumnya berupa manusia, kadang berkepala singa saat membela dewa matahari.",
        "en": "He is usually human, sometimes lion-headed when defending the sun god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c11",
      "source_id": "shu-s2",
      "quote": "The primordial god of the air whose name means \"Emptiness\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 183",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Shu diterjemahkan sebagai Kekosongan.",
        "en": "Shu’s name is translated as Emptiness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c12",
      "source_id": "shu-s2",
      "quote": "The two were gone so long that Atum came to miss them and sent his eye (the Eye of Ra) in search of them.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 183",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu kisah mengisahkan Mata Ra mencari Shu dan Tefnut yang lama pergi.",
        "en": "One tale has the Eye of Ra search for Shu and Tefnut after a long absence."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c13",
      "source_id": "shu-s2",
      "quote": "When the eye returned with them, Atum was so happy he cried and his tears created human beings.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 183",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepulangan mereka membuat Atum menangis bahagia dan air matanya menjadi manusia.",
        "en": "Their return makes Atum weep for joy and his tears become humans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c14",
      "source_id": "shu-s2",
      "quote": "Mist was attributed to him as \"Lakes of Shu\" and the clouds as \"Bones of Shu\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 183",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kabut disebut Danau Shu dalam uraian sumber ini.",
        "en": "Mist is called Lakes of Shu in this source."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shu-c15",
      "source_id": "shu-s3",
      "quote": "To assist him in this work he placed a pillar at each of the cardinal points, and the \"supports of Shu\" are thus the props of the sky.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 120",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge menjelaskan empat pilar di arah mata angin sebagai penopang langit Shu.",
        "en": "Budge describes four pillars at the cardinal points as Shu’s sky supports."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Apophis atau Apep adalah dewa kekacauan Mesir yang biasanya berwujud ular besar. Ia menjadi musuh utama Ra.",
      "en": "Apophis or Apep is an Egyptian chaos deity usually taking the form of a great serpent. He becomes Ra’s greatest enemy.",
