      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia mendapat gelar Musuh Ra dan Penguasa Kekacauan. Beberapa uraian memberikan kepala dari batu api. Asalnya berbeda antar mitos, termasuk dari pusar Ra atau air primordial Nu. Kisah pertempurannya dengan Ra diperluas pada Kerajaan Baru. Sebagian kisah menempatkannya di gunung barat Manu, lainnya menjelang fajar. Tatapan sihirnya dipercaya dapat melemahkan Ra dan rombongannya. Set termasuk pembela Ra dalam pertarungan itu. Doa dan ritual pendeta bertujuan menjamin kemenangan Ra setiap malam. Orang mati yang dibenarkan membantu Ra menghadapi Apep. Ritual kuil bernama Overthrowing of Apophis bertujuan menjaga bahtera matahari. Dalam uraian Budge, Ra menyerang Apep dengan anak panah api. Pengikut Apep juga dibinasakan oleh api. Budge membaca pertarungan itu sebagai gambaran perselisihan harian terang dan gelap.",
      "en": "He bears the titles Enemy of Ra and Lord of Chaos. Some accounts give him a head of flint. His origins vary across myths, including Ra’s umbilical cord or Nu’s primordial waters. Accounts of his battles with Ra expand during the New Kingdom. Some tales place him at western Mount Manu, others just before dawn. His magical gaze is believed to overwhelm Ra and his entourage. Set is among Ra’s defenders in that struggle. Priestly prayers and rituals aim to ensure Ra’s victory each night. The justified dead help Ra confront Apep. The temple ritual Overthrowing of Apophis aims to protect the solar barque. In Budge’s account, Ra attacks Apep with fiery darts. Apep’s attendants are also destroyed by fire. Budge reads that struggle as an image of the daily conflict between light and darkness.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "apophis-c01",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "is the ancient Egyptian deity of chaos, darkness and fire ... usually depicted as a giant snake or serpent.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apophis atau Apep adalah dewa kekacauan Mesir yang biasanya berwujud ular besar.",
        "en": "Apophis or Apep is an Egyptian chaos deity usually taking the form of a great serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c02",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "Because Ra was the solar deity, bringer of light, and thus the upholder of Maat, Apophis was viewed as the greatest enemy of Ra, and thus was given the title Enemy of Ra, and also \"the Lord of Chaos\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi musuh utama Ra.",
        "en": "He becomes Ra’s greatest enemy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c03",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "the title Enemy of Ra, and also \"the Lord of Chaos\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mendapat gelar Musuh Ra dan Penguasa Kekacauan.",
        "en": "He bears the titles Enemy of Ra and Lord of Chaos."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c04",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "Some descriptions said that he stretched 16 yards in length and had a head made of flint.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa uraian memberikan kepala dari batu api.",
        "en": "Some accounts give him a head of flint."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c05",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "usually from his umbilical cord. ... existed from the beginning of time in the waters of Nu of primeval chaos.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asalnya berbeda antar mitos, termasuk dari pusar Ra atau air primordial Nu.",
        "en": "His origins vary across myths, including Ra’s umbilical cord or Nu’s primordial waters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c06",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "Tales of Apophis battles against Ra were elaborated during the New Kingdom. Storytellers said that every day Apophis must lie below the horizon and not persist in the mortal kingdom. This appropriately made him a part of the underworld.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah pertempurannya dengan Ra diperluas pada Kerajaan Baru.",
        "en": "Accounts of his battles with Ra expand during the New Kingdom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c07",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "In some stories, Apophis waited for Ra in a western mountain called Manu, where the sun set, and in others, Apophis lurked just before dawn, in the Tenth region of the Night.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kisah menempatkannya di gunung barat Manu, lainnya menjelang fajar.",
        "en": "Some tales place him at western Mount Manu, others just before dawn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c08",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "The Coffin Texts imply that Apophis used a magical gaze to overwhelm Ra and his entourage. Ra was assisted by a number of defenders who travelled with him, including Set and possibly the Eye of Ra. Apophis' movements were thought to cause earthquakes, and his battles with Set may have been meant to explain the origin of thunderstorms.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tatapan sihirnya dipercaya dapat melemahkan Ra dan rombongannya.",
        "en": "His magical gaze is believed to overwhelm Ra and his entourage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c09",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "Ra was assisted by a number of defenders who travelled with him, including Set and possibly the Eye of Ra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Set termasuk pembela Ra dalam pertarungan itu.",
        "en": "Set is among Ra’s defenders in that struggle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c10",
      "source_id": "apophis-s1",
      "quote": "Ra's victory each night was thought to be ensured by the prayers of the Egyptian priests and worshippers at temples. The Egyptians practiced a number of rituals and superstitions that were thought to ward off Apophis and to aid Ra in continuing his journey across the sky.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Doa dan ritual pendeta bertujuan menjamin kemenangan Ra setiap malam.",
        "en": "Priestly prayers and rituals aim to ensure Ra’s victory each night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c11",
      "source_id": "apophis-s2",
      "quote": "Gods and the justified dead would help Ra fend the serprent off.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 34",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang mati yang dibenarkan membantu Ra menghadapi Apep.",
        "en": "The justified dead help Ra confront Apep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c12",
      "source_id": "apophis-s2",
      "quote": "The ritual known as Overthrowing of Apophis was performed in temples to help the gods and departed souls protect the barge and ensure the coming of day.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 34",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ritual kuil bernama Overthrowing of Apophis bertujuan menjaga bahtera matahari.",
        "en": "The temple ritual Overthrowing of Apophis aims to protect the solar barque."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c13",
      "source_id": "apophis-s3",
      "quote": "the fiery darts which he discharged into the body of =Apep scorched and burnt him up",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 135",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam uraian Budge, Ra menyerang Apep dengan anak panah api.",
        "en": "In Budge’s account, Ra attacks Apep with fiery darts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c14",
      "source_id": "apophis-s3",
      "quote": "the fiends that were in attendance upon this terrible foe were also destroyed by fire",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 135",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengikut Apep juga dibinasakan oleh api.",
        "en": "Apep’s attendants are also destroyed by fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apophis-c15",
      "source_id": "apophis-s3",
      "quote": "it represented originally the fight which was supposed to go on daily between light and darkness.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 135",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge membaca pertarungan itu sebagai gambaran perselisihan harian terang dan gelap.",
        "en": "Budge reads that struggle as an image of the daily conflict between light and darkness."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Khonsu adalah dewa bulan Mesir kuno.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Khonsu adalah dewa bulan Mesir kuno. Namanya berarti pengelana, dihubungkan dengan perjalanan bulan.",
      "en": "Khonsu is an ancient Egyptian moon god. His name means traveller and is connected with the moon’s journey.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "khonsu-c01",
        "khonsu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia membentuk triad Thebes bersama ibu Mut dan ayah Amun. Seni sering menggambarkannya sebagai mumi dengan rambut samping anak-anak. Ia juga dapat berkepala elang dengan cakram dan sabit bulan. Ia disebut dalam Teks Piramida dan Teks Peti Mati. Sebuah kisah Karnak menjadikannya ular besar yang membuahi telur kosmis. Stela Bentresh mengisahkan penyembuhan putri Bekhten setelah gambar Khonsu tiba. Pusat kultusnya mencakup Memphis, Hibis, dan Edfu. Ia dipanggil untuk melindungi dari hewan liar dan membantu penyembuhan. Pada Kerajaan Baru ia disebut sangat populer dan terbesar sesudah Amun. Legenda terjemahan Budge mengisahkan Khonsu Nefer-Hetep mengirim kekuatan pelindung ke bentuk Khonsu lainnya. Raja mengirim gambar Khonsu dalam kapal besar dengan lima kapal pendamping. Perjalanan ke Bekhten berlangsung satu tahun lima bulan dalam legenda tersebut. Pangeran bermimpi Khonsu meninggalkan kuil sebagai elang emas.",
      "en": "He forms the Theban triad with his mother Mut and father Amun. Art often depicts him as a mummy with a child’s sidelock. He may also be falcon-headed with the lunar disk and crescent. He is mentioned in the Pyramid Texts and Coffin Texts. A Karnak account makes him the great serpent fertilizing the cosmic egg. The Bentresh stela recounts healing a princess of Bekhten when Khonsu’s image arrives. His cult locations include Memphis, Hibis and Edfu. He is invoked for protection from wild animals and for healing. In the New Kingdom he is described as highly popular and greatest after Amun. Budge’s translated legend tells of Khonsu Nefer-Hetep transferring protective power to another form of Khonsu. The king sends Khonsu’s image in a great boat with five accompanying boats. The journey to Bekhten lasts one year and five months in that legend. The prince dreams of Khonsu leaving the shrine as a golden hawk.",

  "claims": [

      "statement": {
        "id": "Khonsu adalah dewa bulan Mesir kuno.",
        "en": "Khonsu is an ancient Egyptian moon god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c02",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "His name means 'traveller', and this may relate to the perceived nightly travel of the Moon across the sky.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berarti pengelana, dihubungkan dengan perjalanan bulan.",
        "en": "His name means traveller and is connected with the moon’s journey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c03",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "At Thebes, he formed part of a family triad (the \"Theban Triad\") with Mut his mother and Amun his father.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membentuk triad Thebes bersama ibu Mut dan ayah Amun.",
        "en": "He forms the Theban triad with his mother Mut and father Amun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c04",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "In art, Khonsu is typically depicted as a mummy with the symbol of childhood, a sidelock of hair, as well as the menat necklace with crook and flail. He has close links to other divine children such as Horus and Shu. He was also portrayed with the head of a falcon and like Horus, with whom he is associated as a protector and healer, adorned with the moon disk and crescent moon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seni sering menggambarkannya sebagai mumi dengan rambut samping anak-anak.",
        "en": "Art often depicts him as a mummy with a child’s sidelock."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c05",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "He was also portrayed with the head of a falcon ... adorned with the moon disk and crescent moon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga dapat berkepala elang dengan cakram dan sabit bulan.",
        "en": "He may also be falcon-headed with the lunar disk and crescent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c06",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "Khonsu is mentioned in the Pyramid Texts and Coffin Texts, in which he is depicted in a fierce aspect, but he does not rise to prominence until the New Kingdom, when he is described as the \"Greatest God of the Great Gods\". Most of the construction of the temple complex at Karnak was centered on Khonsu during the Ramesside period.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut dalam Teks Piramida dan Teks Peti Mati.",
        "en": "He is mentioned in the Pyramid Texts and Coffin Texts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c07",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "Khonsu is described as the great snake who fertilizes the Cosmic Egg in the creation of the world.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah kisah Karnak menjadikannya ular besar yang membuahi telur kosmis.",
        "en": "A Karnak account makes him the great serpent fertilizing the cosmic egg."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c08",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "Khonsu's reputation as a healer spread outside Egypt; the Bentresh stela records how a princess of Bekhten was instantly cured of an illness upon the arrival of an image of Khonsu. King Ptolemy IV, after he was cured of an illness, called himself \"Beloved of Khonsu Who Protects His Majesty and Drives Away Evil Spirits\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Stela Bentresh mengisahkan penyembuhan putri Bekhten setelah gambar Khonsu tiba.",

    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c09",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "Locations of Khonsu's cult were Memphis, Hibis, and Edfu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pusat kultusnya mencakup Memphis, Hibis, dan Edfu.",
        "en": "His cult locations include Memphis, Hibis and Edfu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c10",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s1",
      "quote": "Khonsu was invoked to protect against wild animals, and aid with healing.",

    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c11",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s2",
      "quote": "By the time of the New Kingdom (1570-1069 BCE) he was extremely popular and worshipped as the greatest among the gods after Amun.",

      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c12",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s3",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c14",
      "source_id": "khonsu-s3",
      "quote": "this god arrived in Bekhten at the end of a period of one year and five months",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 348",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perjalanan ke Bekhten berlangsung satu tahun lima bulan dalam legenda tersebut.",
        "en": "The journey to Bekhten lasts one year and five months in that legend."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khonsu-c15",

      "quote": "saw this god come forth outside his shrine (now he was in the form of a golden hawk), and he flew up into the heavens and departed to Egypt",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 351",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pangeran bermimpi Khonsu meninggalkan kuil sebagai elang emas.",

    {
      "id": "Neith adalah dewi Mesir yang berhubungan dengan peperangan dan keibuan. Lambang perangnya berupa busur bersilangan.",
      "en": "Neith is an Egyptian goddess associated with warfare and motherhood. Her martial emblem consists of crossed bows.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "neith-c01",
        "neith-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Teks menyebutnya ibu Ra dan Sobek. Dalam sejumlah mitos ia menjadi pencipta dunia dan Ibu Agung. Ia termasuk empat dewi pelindung peti mati dan organ orang wafat. Pusat utama kultusnya adalah Sais di Delta Nil. Orang Yunani mengidentifikasinya dengan Athena. Ia digambarkan memakai Mahkota Merah Mesir Hilir dan memegang busur serta anak panah. Ia sering membawa tongkat was dan ankh. Gelar Pembuka Jalan dapat berhubungan dengan perang maupun penuntunan jiwa. Gelar Nyonya Busur menegaskan hubungannya dengan senjata. Ia menengahi sengketa Horus dan Set mengenai pemerintahan Mesir. Patungnya muncul bersama Isis, Nephthys, dan Serket dalam makam Tutankhamun. Ia menjaga Duamutef, salah satu putra Horus pelindung bejana kanopik. Mackenzie mencatat pengakuan terhadap Neith dalam tradisi tentang Mena.",
      "en": "Texts describe her as the mother of Ra and Sobek. In some myths she becomes creator of the world and Great Mother. She belongs to the four goddesses protecting coffins and the deceased’s internal organs. Her main cult centre is Sais in the Nile Delta. Greek writers identify her with Athena. She is shown with Lower Egypt’s Red Crown and bow and arrows. She often carries a was-sceptre and an ankh. The Opener of the Ways epithet may relate to warfare and guiding souls. The Mistress of the Bow epithet emphasizes her link with weapons. She mediates Horus and Set’s dispute over ruling Egypt. Her statue occurs with Isis, Nephthys and Serket in Tutankhamun’s tomb. She guards Duamutef, a son of Horus protecting canopic jars. Mackenzie records recognition of Neith in the tradition concerning Mena.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "neith-c01",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "was an ancient Egyptian goddess, possibly of Libyan origin. She was associated with warfare",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Neith adalah dewi Mesir yang berhubungan dengan peperangan dan keibuan.",
        "en": "Neith is an Egyptian goddess associated with warfare and motherhood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c02",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "symbolized by her emblem of crossed bows",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lambang perangnya berupa busur bersilangan.",
        "en": "Her martial emblem consists of crossed bows."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c03",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "texts refer to her as the mother of deities such as the sun god Ra and the crocodile god Sobek.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks menyebutnya ibu Ra dan Sobek.",
        "en": "Texts describe her as the mother of Ra and Sobek."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c04",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "As a mother goddess, she was sometimes described as the creator of the world in Egyptian creation myths and referred to as the \"Great Mother\". She also played a role in funerary religion. Over time she became one of the four goddesses who protected the coffin and the internal organs of the deceased.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sejumlah mitos ia menjadi pencipta dunia dan Ibu Agung.",
        "en": "In some myths she becomes creator of the world and Great Mother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c05",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "she became one of the four goddesses who protected the coffin and the internal organs of the deceased.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia termasuk empat dewi pelindung peti mati dan organ orang wafat.",
        "en": "She belongs to the four goddesses protecting coffins and the deceased’s internal organs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c06",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "Her main cult center was the city of Sais in the western Nile Delta.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pusat utama kultusnya adalah Sais di Delta Nil.",
        "en": "Her main cult centre is Sais in the Nile Delta."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c07",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "Greek writers identified her with the goddess Athena.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Yunani mengidentifikasinya dengan Athena.",
        "en": "Greek writers identify her with Athena."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c08",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "Neith is typically depicted as a woman wearing the Red Crown of Lower Egypt and holding a bow and arrows. She is therefore often interpreted as a goddess of war and hunting. Her hieroglyphic symbol consists of two bows crossed over a shield.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan memakai Mahkota Merah Mesir Hilir dan memegang busur serta anak panah.",
        "en": "She is shown with Lower Egypt’s Red Crown and bow and arrows."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c09",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "As a deity, Neith is frequently shown carrying the was-sceptre (a symbol of power) and the ankh (a symbol of life). She was sometimes associated with Mehet-Weret, a cosmic cow goddess who gave birth to the sun daily.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia sering membawa tongkat was dan ankh.",
        "en": "She often carries a was-sceptre and an ankh."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c10",
      "source_id": "neith-s1",
      "quote": "Neith possessed many roles in Egyptian religion. In early texts she was described as an “Opener of the Ways”, an epithet also used for the god Wepwawet. This title may refer to both her role in warfare and her function as a guide of souls in the afterlife.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gelar Pembuka Jalan dapat berhubungan dengan perang maupun penuntunan jiwa.",
        "en": "The Opener of the Ways epithet may relate to warfare and guiding souls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c11",
      "source_id": "neith-s2",
      "quote": "one of her epithets was \"Mistress of the Bow\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 135",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gelar Nyonya Busur menegaskan hubungannya dengan senjata.",
        "en": "The Mistress of the Bow epithet emphasizes her link with weapons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c12",
      "source_id": "neith-s2",
      "quote": "the goddess who settles the question of whether Horus or Set should rule Egypt when the tribunal of the gods cannot decide.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 135",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menengahi sengketa Horus dan Set mengenai pemerintahan Mesir.",
        "en": "She mediates Horus and Set’s dispute over ruling Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c13",
      "source_id": "neith-s2",
      "quote": "Her statue appears with those of Isis, Nephthys, and Serket in Tutankhamun's tomb.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 135",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patungnya muncul bersama Isis, Nephthys, dan Serket dalam makam Tutankhamun.",
        "en": "Her statue occurs with Isis, Nephthys and Serket in Tutankhamun’s tomb."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c14",
      "source_id": "neith-s2",
      "quote": "She is the guardian goddess over Duamutef, one of the Four Sons of Horus who watch over the canopic jars in the tombs",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 135",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjaga Duamutef, salah satu putra Horus pelindung bejana kanopik.",
        "en": "She guards Duamutef, a son of Horus protecting canopic jars."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "neith-c15",
      "source_id": "neith-s3",
      "quote": "Mena also gave diplomatic recognition to Neith, the earth goddess of the Libyans",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 17",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie mencatat pengakuan terhadap Neith dalam tradisi tentang Mena.",
        "en": "Mackenzie records recognition of Neith in the tradition concerning Mena."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Anuket adalah dewi Mesir bagi katarak Nil dan Nubia Hilir.",
    "en": "Anuket is an Egyptian goddess of the Nile cataracts and Lower Nubia.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "anuket-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Anuket adalah dewi Mesir bagi katarak Nil dan Nubia Hilir. Ia terutama dipuja di Elephantine dekat katarak pertama.",
      "en": "Anuket is an Egyptian goddess of the Nile cataracts and Lower Nubia. She is especially worshipped at Elephantine near the First Cataract.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anuket-c01",
        "anuket-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya berarti pemeluk atau perengkuh. Dalam interpretasi Yunani ia disetarakan dengan Hestia atau Vesta. Salah satu hubungan awal menempatkannya sebagai putri Ra. Ia melindungi perbatasan selatan Mesir dan berhubungan dengan banjir Nil. Hubungannya dengan arus cepat turut menghubungkannya dengan papirus. Ia membentuk triad bersama Khnum dan Satis. Kuilnya dibangun di Pulau Seheil. Kultus Elephantine mencakup prosesi sungai pada awal Shemu. Sumber sejarah pendamping menghubungkan Anuket dengan kesuburan dan katarak Aswan. Terjemahan Budge menempatkan Anqet dalam daftar dewa Rumah Ilahi Khnum. Festival Anuket dimulai saat banjir tahunan Nil. Peserta mempersembahkan koin, emas, perhiasan, dan hadiah ke sungai. Larangan memakan ikan tertentu dicabut saat festivalnya.",
      "en": "Her name means Clasper or Embracer. In Greek interpretation she is equated with Hestia or Vesta. One early association makes her a daughter of Ra. She protects Egypt’s southern border and is connected with the Nile flood. Her association with swift water also connects her with papyrus. She forms a triad with Khnum and Satis. Her temple is built on Seheil Island. The Elephantine cult includes a river procession at the start of Shemu. The accompanying historical source connects Anuket with fertility and the Aswan cataract. Budge’s translation places Anqet in the list of gods of Khnum’s Divine House. Anuket’s festival begins with the annual Nile flood. Participants offer coins, gold, jewellery and gifts to the river. The taboo on eating certain fish is lifted during her festival.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anuket-c03",
        "anuket-c04",
        "anuket-c05",
        "anuket-c06",
        "anuket-c07",
        "anuket-c08",
        "anuket-c09",
        "anuket-c10",
        "anuket-c11",
        "anuket-c12",
        "anuket-c13",
        "anuket-c14",
        "anuket-c15"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "anuket-c01",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "Anuket was the ancient Egyptian goddess of the cataracts of the Nile and Lower Nubia in general, worshipped especially at Elephantine near the First Cataract.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anuket adalah dewi Mesir bagi katarak Nil dan Nubia Hilir.",
        "en": "Anuket is an Egyptian goddess of the Nile cataracts and Lower Nubia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c02",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "worshipped especially at Elephantine near the First Cataract.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia terutama dipuja di Elephantine dekat katarak pertama.",
        "en": "She is especially worshipped at Elephantine near the First Cataract."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c03",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "Her name meant the \"Clasper\" or \"Embracer\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berarti pemeluk atau perengkuh.",
        "en": "Her name means Clasper or Embracer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c04",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "In the interpretatio graeca, she was considered equivalent to Hestia or Vesta.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam interpretasi Yunani ia disetarakan dengan Hestia atau Vesta.",
        "en": "In Greek interpretation she is equated with Hestia or Vesta."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c05",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "She was originally the daughter of Ra, but was always related to Satet in some way. For example, both goddesses were called the \"Eye of Ra\", along with Bastet, Hathor, and Sekhmet. Also, they were both related in some way to the Uraeus.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu hubungan awal menempatkannya sebagai putri Ra.",
        "en": "One early association makes her a daughter of Ra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c06",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "Anuket was the goddess of the Nile flood and a protective goddess of the southern border of Egypt. Her posing with her arms outstretched may have been a visual reference to the shape of the Nile, with its two tributaries, and influenced her being called \"the Embracer\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melindungi perbatasan selatan Mesir dan berhubungan dengan banjir Nil.",
        "en": "She protects Egypt’s southern border and is connected with the Nile flood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c07",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "She was associated with swift moving water, and this was the reason for her association with papyruses.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hubungannya dengan arus cepat turut menghubungkannya dengan papirus.",
        "en": "Her association with swift water also connects her with papyrus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c08",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "Anuket was part of a triad with the god Khnum, and the goddess Satis. She may have been the sister of the goddess Satis or she may have been a junior consort to Khnum instead.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membentuk triad bersama Khnum dan Satis.",
        "en": "She forms a triad with Khnum and Satis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c09",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "A temple dedicated to Anuket was erected on the Island of Seheil. Inscriptions show that a shrine or altar was dedicated to her at this site by the 13th Dynasty pharaoh Sobekhotep III. Much later, during the 18th Dynasty, Amenhotep II dedicated a chapel to the goddess.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuilnya dibangun di Pulau Seheil.",
        "en": "Her temple is built on Seheil Island."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c10",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "During the New Kingdom, Anuket's cult at Elephantine included a river procession of the goddess during the first month of Shemu. Inscriptions mention the processional festival of Khnum and Anuket during this period.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kultus Elephantine mencakup prosesi sungai pada awal Shemu.",
        "en": "The Elephantine cult includes a river procession at the start of Shemu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c11",
      "source_id": "anuket-s2",
      "quote": "Anqet (Anukit or Anuket) - Goddess of fertility and the cataract of the Nile River at Aswan.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 29",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber sejarah pendamping menghubungkan Anuket dengan kesuburan dan katarak Aswan.",
        "en": "The accompanying historical source connects Anuket with fertility and the Aswan cataract."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c12",
      "source_id": "anuket-s3",
      "quote": "a list of the names of the gods who dwell in the Divine House of Khnemu. The goddess of the star Sept (Sothis), the goddess Anqet",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 384",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Budge menempatkan Anqet dalam daftar dewa Rumah Ilahi Khnum.",
        "en": "Budge’s translation places Anqet in the list of gods of Khnum’s Divine House."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c13",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "Ceremonially, when the Nile started its annual flood, the Festival of Anuket began.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Festival Anuket dimulai saat banjir tahunan Nil.",
        "en": "Anuket’s festival begins with the annual Nile flood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c14",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "People threw coins, gold, jewelry, and precious gifts into the river",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peserta mempersembahkan koin, emas, perhiasan, dan hadiah ke sungai.",
        "en": "Participants offer coins, gold, jewellery and gifts to the river."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anuket-c15",
      "source_id": "anuket-s1",
      "quote": "The taboo held in several parts of Egypt, against eating certain fish which were considered sacred, was lifted during this time",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Larangan memakan ikan tertentu dicabut saat festivalnya.",
        "en": "The taboo on eating certain fish is lifted during her festival."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Khnum is an Upper Egyptian god originally linked to the Nile cataract.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Khnum adalah dewa Mesir Hulu yang awalnya terkait katarak Nil. Ia mengatur banjir Nil tahunan.",
      "en": "Khnum is an Upper Egyptian god originally linked to the Nile cataract. He regulates the annual Nile inundation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "khnum-c01",
        "khnum-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dianggap pencipta tubuh manusia dan ka. Ia membentuk manusia dari tanah liat dengan roda tembikar, lalu menempatkannya dalam rahim. Ia juga dihormati sebagai pencipta hewan. Pemujaannya berlanjut dari Dinasti Pertama sampai masa Yunani-Romawi. Ia membentuk triad Elephantine bersama Satis dan Anuket. Tanduk domba dalam penggambarannya terkait kesuburan dan kelahiran kembali. Ia memiliki gelar Dewa Perajin Tembikar dan Penguasa Kehidupan. Elephantine dan Esna menjadi dua pusat pemujaannya. Manusia hasil bentukan Khnum diangkat agar cahaya Ra memberi kehidupan. Ia menjadi pelindung perajin tembikar dan keramik. Gelar Perajin Tembikar Agung menegaskan peran penciptanya. Budge mengaitkan Khnum dengan pelaksanaan perintah Thoth bersama Ptah. Dalam kutipan Budge, Khnum disebut ayah para ayah dan ibu para ibu.",
      "en": "He is regarded as creator of human bodies and the ka. He shapes humans from clay on a potter’s wheel and places them in wombs. He is also revered as creator of animals. His worship continues from the First Dynasty to the Greco-Roman period. He forms the Elephantine triad with Satis and Anuket. The ram horns in his representations are linked with fertility and rebirth. He bears the titles Potter God and Lord of Life. Elephantine and Esna are two centres of his worship. Humans shaped by Khnum are raised so Ra’s light gives them life. He is patron of potters and ceramic workers. The Great Potter title emphasizes his creative role. Budge connects Khnum with carrying out Thoth’s commands alongside Ptah. In Budge’s quotation Khnum is called father of fathers and mother of mothers.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "khnum-c01",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "was one of the earliest-known Egyptian deities in Upper Egypt, originally associated with the Nile cataract.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khnum adalah dewa Mesir Hulu yang awalnya terkait katarak Nil.",
        "en": "Khnum is an Upper Egyptian god originally linked to the Nile cataract."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c02",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "He held the responsibility of regulating the annual inundation of the river",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengatur banjir Nil tahunan.",
        "en": "He regulates the annual Nile inundation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c03",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "he eventually became known as the creator of human bodies and the life force kꜣ (\"ka\").",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap pencipta tubuh manusia dan ka.",
        "en": "He is regarded as creator of human bodies and the ka."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c04",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "Using a potter's wheel and clay, he fashioned these entities and placed them within their mothers' wombs.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membentuk manusia dari tanah liat dengan roda tembikar, lalu menempatkannya dalam rahim.",
        "en": "He shapes humans from clay on a potter’s wheel and places them in wombs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c05",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "was revered as the creator of the animal kingdom.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga dihormati sebagai pencipta hewan.",
        "en": "He is also revered as creator of animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c06",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "Worship of Khnum spanned from the First Dynasty and persisted even into the Greco-Roman period when rule by native dynasties had ended. Initially, his primary cult center was at Herwer in Middle Egypt.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya berlanjut dari Dinasti Pertama sampai masa Yunani-Romawi.",
        "en": "His worship continues from the First Dynasty to the Greco-Roman period."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c07",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "At Elephantine, Khnum formed a divine triad alongside the Nubian goddesses Satis and Anuket.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membentuk triad Elephantine bersama Satis dan Anuket.",
        "en": "He forms the Elephantine triad with Satis and Anuket."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c08",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "Khnum's primary function in the inception of human beings was typically portrayed with the horns of a ram, one of the sacred animals worshiped in Ancient Egypt, representing aspects such as fertility, rebirth, regeneration, and resurrection.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tanduk domba dalam penggambarannya terkait kesuburan dan kelahiran kembali.",
        "en": "The ram horns in his representations are linked with fertility and rebirth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c09",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "the \"Creator god\", \"Potter god\", \"Lord of Life\", \"Lord of the Field\", \"Lord of Esna\", \"the good protector\", and \"Lord of the crocodiles\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memiliki gelar Dewa Perajin Tembikar dan Penguasa Kehidupan.",
        "en": "He bears the titles Potter God and Lord of Life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c10",
      "source_id": "khnum-s1",
      "quote": "The worship of Khnum centered on two principal riverside sites, Elephantine and Esna, which were regarded as sacred sites. At Elephantine, he was worshipped alongside Satis and Anuket, while at Esna, he was worshipped alongside Menhit, Nebtu, Neith and Heka. Banebdjedet was the equivalent god in Lower Egypt. Khnum has also been related to the deity Min.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Elephantine dan Esna menjadi dua pusat pemujaannya.",
        "en": "Elephantine and Esna are two centres of his worship."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c11",
      "source_id": "khnum-s2",
      "quote": "then held them high so the light of Ra could shine upon them and give them life.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 105",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Manusia hasil bentukan Khnum diangkat agar cahaya Ra memberi kehidupan.",
        "en": "Humans shaped by Khnum are raised so Ra’s light gives them life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c12",
      "source_id": "khnum-s2",
      "quote": "He is the patron god of potters and those who work in ceramics.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 105",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi pelindung perajin tembikar dan keramik.",
        "en": "He is patron of potters and ceramic workers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c13",
      "source_id": "khnum-s2",
      "quote": "Also known as \"The Great Potter\", Khenmu was an early god of Upper Egypt",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 105",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gelar Perajin Tembikar Agung menegaskan peran penciptanya.",
        "en": "The Great Potter title emphasizes his creative role."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khnum-c14",
      "source_id": "khnum-s3",
      "quote": "KHNEMU was one of the old cosmic gods who assisted Ptah in carrying out the commands of Thoth",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 133",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Khepri adalah dewa berwajah kumbang skarab yang mewakili matahari terbit dalam agama Mesir.",
    "en": "Khepri is a scarab-faced god representing the rising sun in Egyptian religion.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "khepri-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Khepri adalah dewa berwajah kumbang skarab yang mewakili matahari terbit dalam agama Mesir. Ia juga mewakili penciptaan dan pembaruan kehidupan.",
      "en": "Khepri is a scarab-faced god representing the rising sun in Egyptian religion. He also represents creation and renewal of life.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "khepri-c01",
        "khepri-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya disebut dalam Teks Piramida dan Amduat. Namanya berasal dari kata yang berarti berkembang atau mencipta. Kemunculan kumbang dari bola kotoran mendorong kaitan dengan penciptaan dari ketiadaan. Kelahiran matahari setiap hari dikaitkan dengan skarab. Pada jam kedua belas Amduat, Khepri mengemudikan bahtera matahari. Perhiasan berbentuk skarab dipakai sebagai cincin dan jimat. Ia dianggap sebagai manifestasi Ra, bukan pusat kultus tersendiri. Khepri mewakili pagi, Ra tengah hari, dan Atum senja. Ensiklopedia pendamping menyebut Khepri aspek pagi Ra. Budge membaca Khepera sebagai materi yang menyimpan benih kehidupan baru. Penafsiran itu juga menghubungkan tubuh mati dengan tubuh spiritual yang bangkit. Budge mencatat mitos skarab keluar dari lubang hidung kepala Osiris di Abydos. Dalam legenda terjemahan Budge, Neb-er-tcher berkata ia muncul sebagai Khepera.",
      "en": "His name appears in the Pyramid Texts and Amduat. His name derives from a word meaning develop or create. Beetles emerging from dung balls inspire the association with creation from nothing. The sun’s daily rebirth is associated with the scarab. In the Amduat’s twelfth hour Khepri steers the solar barque. Scarab-shaped jewellery is used as rings and amulets. He is regarded as a manifestation of Ra rather than a separate cult focus. Khepri represents morning, Ra midday and Atum evening. The accompanying encyclopedia identifies Khepri as Ra’s morning aspect. Budge reads Khepera as matter containing the germ of new life. That interpretation also links the dead body with the rising spiritual body. Budge records a myth of a scarab emerging from the nostrils of Osiris’s head at Abydos. In Budge’s translated legend, Neb-er-tcher says he comes into being as Khepera.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "khepri-c03",
        "khepri-c04",
        "khepri-c05",
        "khepri-c06",
        "khepri-c07",
        "khepri-c08",
        "khepri-c09",
        "khepri-c10",
        "khepri-c11",
        "khepri-c12",
        "khepri-c13",
        "khepri-c14",
        "khepri-c15"
      ]
    }

      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "is a scarab-faced god in ancient Egyptian religion who represents the rising or morning sun.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khepri adalah dewa berwajah kumbang skarab yang mewakili matahari terbit dalam agama Mesir.",
        "en": "Khepri is a scarab-faced god representing the rising sun in Egyptian religion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c02",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "By extension, he can also represent creation and the renewal of life.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga mewakili penciptaan dan pembaruan kehidupan.",
        "en": "He also represents creation and renewal of life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c03",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "The name \"Khepri\" appeared in the Pyramid texts and usually included the scarab hieroglyph as a determinative or ideogram as a potential means to make any allusions to the god clear. Khepri is also mentioned in the Amduat, as the god is intrinsically linked to cycle of the sun and Ra's nightly journey through the Duat, the Egyptian underworld.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya disebut dalam Teks Piramida dan Amduat.",
        "en": "His name appears in the Pyramid Texts and Amduat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c04",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "Khepri (ḫprj) is derived from the Egyptian language verb ḫpr, meaning to \"develop\" or \"create\". Khepri (ḫprj) can also be spelled \"Kheper\", which is the Egyptian term used to denote the sun god, the scarab beetle, and the verbs \"to come into existence\" or \"to be born\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berasal dari kata yang berarti berkembang atau mencipta.",
        "en": "His name derives from a word meaning develop or create."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c05",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "This caused ancient Egyptians to believe that these insects were created from nothingness.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kemunculan kumbang dari bola kotoran mendorong kaitan dengan penciptaan dari ketiadaan.",
        "en": "Beetles emerging from dung balls inspire the association with creation from nothing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c06",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "They also believed that each day the sun was reborn or created from nothing, thus explaining the connection made between the Sun and the scarab.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kelahiran matahari setiap hari dikaitkan dengan skarab.",
        "en": "The sun’s daily rebirth is associated with the scarab."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c07",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "In hour twelve of the Amduat, a newly reborn Khepri helms the solar barque that pushes the sun, moving the morning sun across the early day sky. This mirrors the manner in which a scarab beetle pushes large balls of dung along the ground, highlighting the relationship made between Khepri and the insects.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada jam kedua belas Amduat, Khepri mengemudikan bahtera matahari.",
        "en": "In the Amduat’s twelfth hour Khepri steers the solar barque."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c08",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "Scarab-shaped accessories were common in ancient Egypt, as rings or amulets meant to be attached to necklaces were often fashioned in the shape of these insects.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perhiasan berbentuk skarab dipakai sebagai cincin dan jimat.",
        "en": "Scarab-shaped jewellery is used as rings and amulets."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c09",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "There was no cult devoted to Khepri, as he was seen as a manifestation of the more prominent solar deity Ra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap sebagai manifestasi Ra, bukan pusat kultus tersendiri.",
        "en": "He is regarded as a manifestation of Ra rather than a separate cult focus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c10",
      "source_id": "khepri-s1",
      "quote": "Khepri represented the morning sun, Ra was the midday sun, and Atum represented the evening sun.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khepri mewakili pagi, Ra tengah hari, dan Atum senja.",
        "en": "Khepri represents morning, Ra midday and Atum evening."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c11",
      "source_id": "khepri-s2",
      "quote": "Khepri - An aspect of Ra the sun god in his morning form, represented by the scarab beetle.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 106",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ensiklopedia pendamping menyebut Khepri aspek pagi Ra.",
        "en": "The accompanying encyclopedia identifies Khepri as Ra’s morning aspect."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c12",
      "source_id": "khepri-s3",
      "quote": "the type of matter which contains within itself the germ of life which is about to spring into a new existence",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 134",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge membaca Khepera sebagai materi yang menyimpan benih kehidupan baru.",
        "en": "Budge reads Khepera as matter containing the germ of new life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c13",
      "source_id": "khepri-s3",
      "quote": "thus he represented the dead body from which the spiritual body was about to rise.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 134",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penafsiran itu juga menghubungkan tubuh mati dengan tubuh spiritual yang bangkit.",
        "en": "That interpretation also links the dead body with the rising spiritual body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c14",
      "source_id": "khepri-s3",
      "quote": "from the nostrils of the head of Osiris, which was buried at Abydos, came forth the scarabaeus",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 82",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge mencatat mitos skarab keluar dari lubang hidung kepala Osiris di Abydos.",
        "en": "Budge records a myth of a scarab emerging from the nostrils of Osiris’s head at Abydos."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khepri-c15",
      "source_id": "khepri-s4",
      "quote": "I am he who came into being in the form of the god Khepera, and I am the creator of that which came into being",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 163",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda terjemahan Budge, Neb-er-tcher berkata ia muncul sebagai Khepera.",
        "en": "In Budge’s translated legend, Neb-er-tcher says he comes into being as Khepera."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "en": "Apis is a sacred bull worshipped in the Memphis region of Egyptian religion.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Apis adalah banteng suci yang dipuja di daerah Memphis dalam agama Mesir. Ia dikenal sebagai putra Hathor.",
      "en": "Apis is a sacred bull worshipped in the Memphis region of Egyptian religion. He is known as Hathor’s son.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "apis-c01",
        "apis-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia menjadi perantara manusia dengan dewa, awalnya Ptah. Penggalian Serapeum Saqqara menemukan makam lebih dari enam puluh hewan. Pemujaan banteng Apis diketahui sejak Dinasti Pertama di Memphis. Setelah mati, Apis menjadi Osorapis atau Osiris-Apis. Kultus Apis termasuk tiga kultus banteng besar bersama Mnevis dan Buchis. Banteng melambangkan keberanian, kekuatan, dan daya juang raja. Ia kadang digambarkan dengan cakram matahari di antara tanduknya. Banteng terpilih dikenali melalui tanda khusus termasuk segitiga putih di dahi. Kultus Apis disebut salah satu yang penting dan berumur panjang dalam sejarah Mesir. Mackenzie mencatat keyakinan bahwa Apis lahir melalui cahaya generatif bulan. Gerakan Apis ditafsirkan sebagai nubuat di kuil. Napas Apis dipercaya menyembuhkan penyakit. Pada hari tertentu ia diarak dengan perhiasan dan bunga.",
      "en": "He mediates between humans and gods, originally Ptah. Excavation of Saqqara’s Serapeum reveals tombs of over sixty animals. Worship of an Apis bull is known from the First Dynasty at Memphis. After death Apis becomes Osorapis or Osiris-Apis. The Apis cult belongs to three major bull cults with Mnevis and Buchis. The bull symbolizes the king’s courage, strength and fighting spirit. He is sometimes shown with the solar disk between his horns. The chosen bull is identified by special markings including a white forehead triangle. The Apis cult is described as an important and long-lived cult in Egyptian history. Mackenzie records the belief that Apis is born through generative moonlight. Apis’s movements are interpreted as prophecies at the temple. Apis’s breath is believed to cure disease. On certain holidays he is paraded with jewellery and flowers.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "apis-c01",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apis adalah banteng suci yang dipuja di daerah Memphis dalam agama Mesir.",

    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c02",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "identified as the son of Hathor, a primary deity in the pantheon of ancient Egypt.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikenal sebagai putra Hathor.",
        "en": "He is known as Hathor’s son."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c03",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "Apis also served as an intermediary between humans and other powerful deities (originally Ptah, later Osiris, then Atum).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi perantara manusia dengan dewa, awalnya Ptah.",
        "en": "He mediates between humans and gods, originally Ptah."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c04",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "Auguste Mariette's excavation of the Serapeum of Saqqara revealed the tombs of more than sixty animals",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggalian Serapeum Saqqara menemukan makam lebih dari enam puluh hewan.",
        "en": "Excavation of Saqqara’s Serapeum reveals tombs of over sixty animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c05",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "Worship of an Apis bull, experienced by ancient Egyptians as holy, has been known since the First Dynasty in Memphis, while worship of the Apis as a proper god, at least according to Manetho's Aegyptiaca, seems to be a later adoption, purportedly started during the reign of king Kaiechos (possibly Nebra) of the Second Dynasty.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan banteng Apis diketahui sejak Dinasti Pertama di Memphis.",
        "en": "Worship of an Apis bull is known from the First Dynasty at Memphis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c06",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "after death, he became Osorapis, i.e. the Osiris Apis",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah mati, Apis menjadi Osorapis atau Osiris-Apis.",
        "en": "After death Apis becomes Osorapis or Osiris-Apis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c07",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "Apis was the most popular of three great bull cults of ancient Egypt, the others being the cults of Mnevis and Buchis. All are related to the worship of Hathor or Bat, similar primary goddesses separated by region until unification that eventually merged as Hathor. The worship of Apis was continued by the Greeks and after them by the Romans, and lasted until almost 400 CE.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kultus Apis termasuk tiga kultus banteng besar bersama Mnevis dan Buchis.",
        "en": "The Apis cult belongs to three major bull cults with Mnevis and Buchis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c08",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "This animal was chosen because it symbolized the courageous heart, great strength, and fighting spirit of the king. Apis came to be considered a manifestation of the king, as bulls were symbols of strength and fertility, qualities that are closely linked with kingship.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banteng melambangkan keberanian, kekuatan, dan daya juang raja.",
        "en": "The bull symbolizes the king’s courage, strength and fighting spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c09",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "Occasionally, Apis was pictured with the sun-disk symbol of his mother, Hathor, between his horns, being one of few deities ever associated with her symbol. When the disk was depicted on his head with his horns below and the triangular marking on his forehead, an ankh was suggested. That symbol always was closely associated with Hathor.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia kadang digambarkan dengan cakram matahari di antara tanduknya.",
        "en": "He is sometimes shown with the solar disk between his horns."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c10",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "It was required to have a white triangular marking upon its forehead",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banteng terpilih dikenali melalui tanda khusus termasuk segitiga putih di dahi.",
        "en": "The chosen bull is identified by special markings including a white forehead triangle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c11",
      "source_id": "apis-s2",
      "quote": "The Apis Cult was one of the most important and long-lived in the history of Egyptian culture.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 35",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kultus Apis disebut salah satu yang penting dan berumur panjang dalam sejarah Mesir.",
        "en": "The Apis cult is described as an important and long-lived cult in Egyptian history."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c12",
      "source_id": "apis-s3",
      "quote": "It was begotten\", Plutarch was informed, \"by a ray of generative light flowing from the moon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 23",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie mencatat keyakinan bahwa Apis lahir melalui cahaya generatif bulan.",
        "en": "Mackenzie records the belief that Apis is born through generative moonlight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c13",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "At the temple, Apis was used as an oracle, his movements being interpreted as prophecies.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 10",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gerakan Apis ditafsirkan sebagai nubuat di kuil.",
        "en": "Apis’s movements are interpreted as prophecies at the temple."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c14",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "His breath was believed to cure disease and his presence to bless those around with strength.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 10",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Napas Apis dipercaya menyembuhkan penyakit.",
        "en": "Apis’s breath is believed to cure disease."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apis-c15",
      "source_id": "apis-s1",
      "quote": "he was led through the streets of the city, bedecked with jewelry and flowers.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 10",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada hari tertentu ia diarak dengan perhiasan dan bunga.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bes adalah dewa Mesir pelindung rumah tangga, ibu, anak, dan kelahiran.",
    "en": "Bes is an Egyptian deity protecting households, mothers, children and childbirth.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bes-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bes adalah dewa Mesir pelindung rumah tangga, ibu, anak, dan kelahiran. Beset adalah pasangan femininnya.",
      "en": "Bes is an Egyptian deity protecting households, mothers, children and childbirth. Beset is his feminine counterpart.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bes-c01",
        "bes-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemujaannya menyebar ke Siria dan Kepulauan Balearik. Ia menjaga anak dan menghadapi roh jahat saat perempuan melahirkan. Berbeda dari kebanyakan dewa Mesir, gambarnya menghadap depan. Patungnya dipasang di rumah sebagai perlindungan terhadap setan. Bes juga mewakili musik, tari, dan kenikmatan seksual. Gambarnya terdapat pada masker, jimat, botol bayi, dan pisau magis. Ruangan Saqqara bergambar Bes dan Beset dikaitkan dengan ritual penyembuhan. Gambar Bes ditemukan di Susa dan Asia Tengah. Bes digambarkan berjanggut, bertelinga besar, berkaki bengkok, dan menggoyang alat bunyi. Sumber ini menyebut Taweret sebagai pasangannya. Perabot, cermin, dan gagang pisau rumah tangga memuat gambarnya. Mackenzie menyebut orakel Bes di Abydos masih dikenal pada masa Romawi. Dalam uraian Mackenzie, Bes mengoyak ular yang menyerang dewa anak.",
      "en": "His worship spreads to Syria and the Balearic Islands. He watches over children and fights evil spirits during childbirth. Unlike most Egyptian gods, his image faces forward. His statue is placed in homes for protection against demons. Bes also symbolizes music, dance and sexual pleasure. His image occurs on masks, amulets, infant bottles and magical knives. Rooms at Saqqara showing Bes and Beset are associated with healing rituals. Images of Bes are found at Susa and in Central Asia. Bes is shown bearded, large-eared, bow-legged and shaking a rattle. This source names Taweret as his consort. Household furniture, mirrors and knife handles bear his image. Mackenzie states that Bes’s oracle at Abydos is still known in Roman times. In Mackenzie’s account, Bes tears apart serpents attacking the child god.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "bes-c01",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "is an ancient Egyptian deity ... worshipped as a protector of households and, in particular, of mothers, children, and childbirth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bes adalah dewa Mesir pelindung rumah tangga, ibu, anak, dan kelahiran.",
        "en": "Bes is an Egyptian deity protecting households, mothers, children and childbirth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c02",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "together with his feminine counterpart Beset",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beset adalah pasangan femininnya.",
        "en": "Beset is his feminine counterpart."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c03",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "Worship of Bes spread as far north as the area of Syria and as far west as the Balearic Islands (Ibiza) in Spain, and later into the Roman and Achaemenid Empires.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya menyebar ke Siria dan Kepulauan Balearik.",
        "en": "His worship spreads to Syria and the Balearic Islands."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c04",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "Bes was a household protector, becoming responsible– throughout ancient Egyptian history– for such varied tasks as killing snakes, fighting off evil spirits, watching after children, and aiding women in labour by fighting off evil spirits, and thus present with Taweret at births.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjaga anak dan menghadapi roh jahat saat perempuan melahirkan.",
        "en": "He watches over children and fights evil spirits during childbirth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c05",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "Images of the deity, quite different from those of the other gods, were kept in homes. Normally Egyptian gods were shown in profile, but instead Bes appeared in full face portrait, ithyphallic, and sometimes in a soldier's tunic, so as to appear ready to launch an attack on any approaching evil. He scared away demons from houses, so his statue was put up as a protector.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbeda dari kebanyakan dewa Mesir, gambarnya menghadap depan.",
        "en": "Unlike most Egyptian gods, his image faces forward."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c06",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "He scared away demons from houses, so his statue was put up as a protector.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patungnya dipasang di rumah sebagai perlindungan terhadap setan.",
        "en": "His statue is placed in homes for protection against demons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c07",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "Bes also came to symbolize the good things in life– music, dance, and sexual pleasure.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bes juga mewakili musik, tari, dan kenikmatan seksual.",
        "en": "Bes also symbolizes music, dance and sexual pleasure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c08",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "Bes is depicted on a variety of objects, including masks, amulets, infant feeding bottles, and magic knives.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambarnya terdapat pada masker, jimat, botol bayi, dan pisau magis.",
        "en": "His image occurs on masks, amulets, infant bottles and magical knives."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c09",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "Later, in the Ptolemaic period of Egyptian history, chambers were constructed at Saqqara, painted with images of Bes and his female counterpart Beset, thought by Egyptologists to have been for the purpose of curing fertility problems or general healing rituals.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ruangan Saqqara bergambar Bes dan Beset dikaitkan dengan ritual penyembuhan.",
        "en": "Rooms at Saqqara showing Bes and Beset are associated with healing rituals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c10",
      "source_id": "bes-s1",
      "quote": "At the end of the 6th century BC, images of Bes began to spread across the Achaemenid Empire, which Egypt belonged to at the time. Images of Bes have been found at the Persian capital of Susa, and as far away as central Asia. Over time, the image of Bes became more Persian in style, as he was depicted wearing Persian clothes and headdress.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambar Bes ditemukan di Susa dan Asia Tengah.",
        "en": "Images of Bes are found at Susa and in Central Asia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c11",
      "source_id": "bes-s2",
      "quote": "Bes is depicted as a bearded dwarf with large ears, prominent genitals, bow-legged, and shaking a rattle.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 51",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bes digambarkan berjanggut, bertelinga besar, berkaki bengkok, dan menggoyang alat bunyi.",
        "en": "Bes is shown bearded, large-eared, bow-legged and shaking a rattle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c12",
      "source_id": "bes-s2",
      "quote": "His consort was Taweret, the hippopotamus goddess of childbirth and fertility.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 51",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber ini menyebut Taweret sebagai pasangannya.",
        "en": "This source names Taweret as his consort."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bes-c13",
      "source_id": "bes-s2",
      "quote": "featured on a number of everyday items in the homes of the Egyptians such as furniture, mirrors, and knife handles.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 51",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perabot, cermin, dan gagang pisau rumah tangga memuat gambarnya.",

    },
    {

      "source_id": "bes-s3",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hapi adalah dewa banjir tahunan Nil dalam agama Mesir. Banjirnya membawa endapan subur yang mendukung pertanian.",
      "en": "Hapi is the god of the annual Nile flood in Egyptian religion. His flood brings fertile silt supporting agriculture.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hapi-c01",
        "hapi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia memiliki gelar Penguasa Sungai Pembawa Tumbuhan. Ia digambarkan berperut besar, berdada menonjol, dan berjanggut palsu. Banjir tahunan kadang disebut Kedatangan Hapi. Ia dipercaya tinggal di gua dekat Aswan. Pendeta menggunakan nilometer Elephantine untuk memantau tinggi banjir. Ia adalah dewa peristiwa banjir, bukan dewa sungai Nil itu sendiri. Kulit biru atau hijaunya melambangkan air. Sepasang Hapi yang mengikat tanaman melambangkan persatuan Mesir Hulu dan Hilir. Ensiklopedia ini menghubungkan dada dan perut Hapi dengan kesuburan serta keberhasilan. Himne terjemahan Budge memuji Hapi karena mengairi ladang dan menjaga hewan hidup. Himne yang sama memanggilnya pemberi gandum. Budge menjelaskan beberapa gambar Hapi sebagai dua dewa dengan lambang papirus dan teratai. Budge mencatat himne yang menyebut Hapi menciptakan dirinya sendiri.",
      "en": "He bears the title Lord of the River Bringing Vegetation. He is shown with a prominent belly, drooping breasts and false beard. The annual flood is sometimes called the Arrival of Hapi. He is believed to live in a cavern near Aswan. Priests use Elephantine’s nilometer to monitor flood levels. He is the god of the inundation event rather than the Nile itself. His blue or green skin represents water. Paired Hapi figures tying plants symbolize the union of Upper and Lower Egypt. This encyclopedia connects Hapi’s breasts and belly with fertility and success. The hymn translated by Budge praises Hapi for watering fields and sustaining animals. The same hymn calls him giver of grain. Budge explains some images of Hapi as two gods with papyrus and lotus emblems. Budge records a hymn saying that Hapi creates himself.",

  "claims": [
    {

      "quote": "was the god of the annual flooding of the Nile in ancient Egyptian religion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hapi adalah dewa banjir tahunan Nil dalam agama Mesir.",
