    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c02",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "The flood deposited rich silt on the river's banks, fertilizing the soil and enabling the Egyptians to grow crops.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banjirnya membawa endapan subur yang mendukung pertanian.",
        "en": "His flood brings fertile silt supporting agriculture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c03",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "\"Lord of the Fish and Birds of the Marshes\" and \"Lord of the River Bringing Vegetation\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memiliki gelar Penguasa Sungai Pembawa Tumbuhan.",
        "en": "He bears the title Lord of the River Bringing Vegetation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c04",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "Hapi is typically depicted as an androgynous figure with a prominent belly and large drooping breasts, wearing a loincloth and ceremonial false beard.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan berperut besar, berdada menonjol, dan berjanggut palsu.",
        "en": "He is shown with a prominent belly, drooping breasts and false beard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c05",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "The annual flooding of the Nile occasionally was said to be the Arrival of Hapi. Since this flooding provided fertile soil in an area that was otherwise desert, Hapi symbolised fertility. He had large female breasts because he was said to bring a rich and nourishing harvest.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banjir tahunan kadang disebut Kedatangan Hapi.",
        "en": "The annual flood is sometimes called the Arrival of Hapi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c06",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "He was thought to live within a cavern at the supposed source of the Nile near Aswan.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipercaya tinggal di gua dekat Aswan.",
        "en": "He is believed to live in a cavern near Aswan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c07",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "At Elephantine the official nilometer, a measuring device, was carefully monitored to predict the level of the flood",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pendeta menggunakan nilometer Elephantine untuk memantau tinggi banjir.",
        "en": "Priests use Elephantine’s nilometer to monitor flood levels."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c08",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "Hapi was not regarded as the god of the Nile itself but of the inundation event. He was also considered a \"friend of Geb\", the Egyptian god of the earth, and the \"lord of Neper\", the god of grain.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah dewa peristiwa banjir, bukan dewa sungai Nil itu sendiri.",
        "en": "He is the god of the inundation event rather than the Nile itself."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c09",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "He was usually given blue or green skin, representing water.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kulit biru atau hijaunya melambangkan air.",
        "en": "His blue or green skin represents water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hapi-c10",
      "source_id": "hapi-s1",
      "quote": "each holding and tying together the long stem of two plants representing Upper and Lower Egypt, symbolically binding the two halves of the country",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      "statement": {

    },
    {

    {
      "id": "Pusat kultusnya adalah Coptos dan Akhmim di Mesir Hulu. Ia berhubungan dengan gurun timur dan Horus. Sinkretisme menghasilkannya sebagai Min-Horus dan Min-Amun.     "en": "Amunet is a primordial goddess in ancient Egyptian religion.",

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Amunet adalah dewi primordial dalam agama Mesir kuno. Thebes menjadi pusat pemujaannya.",
      "en": "Amunet is a primordial goddess in ancient Egyptian religion. Thebes is the centre of her worship.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "amunet-c01",
        "amunet-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia berpasangan dengan Amun sebelum penciptaan. Namanya merupakan kata feminin yang berarti Yang Tersembunyi. Ia anggota Ogdoad Hermopolis yang melambangkan keadaan sebelum penciptaan. Teks Piramida menyebut bayangan Amun dan Amunet. Mut sering menggantikannya sebagai pasangan Amun sejak setidaknya Dinasti Kedua Belas. Ia tetap menjadi pelindung raja dalam ritual penobatan dan festival Sed. Ia lebih sering tampil bersama Amun daripada bersama anak dewa seperti Khonsu. Kultusnya memiliki sedikit pengikut di luar Thebes. Ensiklopedia pendamping menegaskan keanggotaan Amunet dalam Ogdoad. Kajian Hypostyle Hall mengenali Amunet sebagai dewi bermakhota merah pada jamb selatan. Satu relief menunjukkan Ramses II menyebar gipsum di depan Amun-Re dan Amunet. Kajian itu juga mencatat adegan Seti I di depan Amunet. Teks ucapan Amunet dalam relief memberinya peran menyediakan pangan.",
      "en": "She is paired with Amun before creation. Her name is a feminine noun meaning the Hidden One. She is a member of Hermopolis’s Ogdoad representing existence before creation. The Pyramid Texts mention the shadow of Amun and Amunet. Mut often supersedes her as Amun’s partner from at least the Twelfth Dynasty. She remains a protector of the king in coronation rites and Sed festivals. She more often appears with Amun than with divine children such as Khonsu. Her cult has relatively few followers outside Thebes. The accompanying encyclopedia confirms Amunet’s membership of the Ogdoad. The Hypostyle Hall study identifies Amunet as a Red-Crowned goddess on the south jamb. One relief shows Ramesses II scattering gypsum before Amun-Re and Amunet. The study also records a scene of Sety I before Amunet. Amunet’s speech in the relief gives her a role of providing food.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "amunet-c03",
        "amunet-c04",
        "amunet-c05",
        "amunet-c06",
        "amunet-c07",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "amunet-c01",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "is a primordial goddess in ancient Egyptian religion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Amunet adalah dewi primordial dalam agama Mesir kuno.",
        "en": "Amunet is a primordial goddess in ancient Egyptian religion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c02",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "Thebes was the center of her worship",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Thebes menjadi pusat pemujaannya.",
        "en": "Thebes is the centre of her worship."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c03",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "was paired with a counterpart who is entitled with the same name, but in the masculine, Amun. They were thought to have existed prior to the beginning of creation",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berpasangan dengan Amun sebelum penciptaan.",
        "en": "She is paired with Amun before creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c04",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "Her name, jmnt, is a feminine noun that means \"The Hidden One\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya merupakan kata feminin yang berarti Yang Tersembunyi.",
        "en": "Her name is a feminine noun meaning the Hidden One."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c05",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "As a member of the Ogdoad of Hermopolis, she represented aspects of the primeval existence before the creation",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia anggota Ogdoad Hermopolis yang melambangkan keadaan sebelum penciptaan.",
        "en": "She is a member of Hermopolis’s Ogdoad representing existence before creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c06",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "The Pyramid Texts, the earliest known religious texts of Ancient Egypt, mention \"the beneficent shadow of Amun and Amunet\":",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Piramida menyebut bayangan Amun dan Amunet.",
        "en": "The Pyramid Texts mention the shadow of Amun and Amunet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c07",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "By at least the Twelfth Dynasty of Egypt (c. 1991–1803 BC), Amunet often was superseded by Mut as Amun's partner, as cults evolved or similar ones in other regions were merged following Mentuhotep II's reunification of Egypt—but Amunet remained locally important in Thebes, where Amun was worshipped.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mut sering menggantikannya sebagai pasangan Amun sejak setidaknya Dinasti Kedua Belas.",
        "en": "Mut often supersedes her as Amun’s partner from at least the Twelfth Dynasty."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c08",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "she was seen as a protector of the king, playing a preeminent role in rituals associated with the royal coronation (khaj-nisut) and Sed festivals (heb-sed)",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tetap menjadi pelindung raja dalam ritual penobatan dan festival Sed.",
        "en": "She remains a protector of the king in coronation rites and Sed festivals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c09",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "Amunet appears most frequently alongside ithyphallic forms of Amun such as Amenope or Amun-Min-kamutef and never appears in temple scenes together with Khonsu or any other child gods. Thus while Amun, Mut and Khonsu form the classic Theban triad, Amunet only accompanies Amun. In the Festival Hall of Thutmose III (c.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia lebih sering tampil bersama Amun daripada bersama anak dewa seperti Khonsu.",
        "en": "She more often appears with Amun than with divine children such as Khonsu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c10",
      "source_id": "amunet-s1",
      "quote": "In spite of Amunet's stable position as a local goddess of Egypt's most important city, her cult began to have very little following outside the Theban region that developed into a dominant religious center for the unified country.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kultusnya memiliki sedikit pengikut di luar Thebes.",
        "en": "Her cult has relatively few followers outside Thebes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "amunet-c11",
      "source_id": "amunet-s2",

    {
      "id": "Serapis adalah dewa Yunani-Mesir yang memadukan Osiris dan Apis. Ptolemaios I memopulerkannya untuk menyatukan rakyat Yunani dan Mesir.",
      "en": "Serapis is a Graeco-Egyptian god combining Osiris and Apis. Ptolemy I popularizes him to unite Greek and Egyptian subjects.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "serapis-c01",
        "serapis-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Raja Ptolemaios berikutnya menyebarkan kultus itu sebagai kebijakan. Pada masa Romawi ia sering menggantikan Osiris sebagai pasangan Isis di luar Mesir. Namanya berasal dari pemujaan gabungan Osiris-Apis. Sarapis merupakan bentuk Yunani awal, sedangkan Serapis menjadi umum pada masa Romawi. Serapeum adalah kuil atau kawasan ibadah untuk Serapis. Serapeum paling terkenal berada di Alexandria. Penggambarannya memadukan penampilan dewa Yunani dengan atribut Mesir. Patung kultus Alexandria menunjukkan figur bertahta dengan ukuran biji-bijian di kepala dan Cerberus di kaki. Sumber ini menghubungkan sifat Serapis dengan Zeus, Helios, Dionysos, Hades, dan Asklepios. Ia menjadi dewa tertinggi yang dipuja di Serapeum dekat Perpustakaan Alexandria. Kultusnya mendukung gagasan masyarakat multibudaya Ptolemaios I. Sumber ini memandang Serapis sebagai paduan ideal Mesir dan Yunani. Mackenzie menyalin undangan makan di meja Serapis dalam Serapeum.",
      "en": "Later Ptolemaic kings spread the cult as policy. In Roman times he often replaces Osiris as Isis’s consort outside Egypt. His name derives from the combined worship of Osiris-Apis. Sarapis is the earlier Greek form, while Serapis becomes common in Roman times. A serapeum is a temple or religious precinct dedicated to Serapis. The best-known serapeum is at Alexandria. His depictions combine Greek divine appearance with Egyptian attributes. The Alexandrian cult statue shows an enthroned figure with a grain measure on his head and Cerberus at his feet. This source connects Serapis’s attributes with Zeus, Helios, Dionysus, Hades and Asclepius. He becomes the supreme deity worshipped at the Serapeum near Alexandria’s Library. His cult supports Ptolemy I’s idea of a multicultural society. This source views Serapis as a blend of Egyptian and Greek ideals.     {
      "id": "serapis-c01",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "Serapis or Sarapis is a Graeco-Egyptian god. A syncretic deity derived from the worship of the Egyptian gods Osiris and Apis, Serapis was extensively popularized in the third century BC on the orders of Greek Pharaoh Ptolemy I Soter, as a means to unify the Greek and Egyptian subjects of the Ptolemaic Kingdom.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Serapis adalah dewa Yunani-Mesir yang memadukan Osiris dan Apis.",
        "en": "Serapis is a Graeco-Egyptian god combining Osiris and Apis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c02",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "Serapis was extensively popularized in the third century BC on the orders of Greek Pharaoh Ptolemy I Soter, as a means to unify the Greek and Egyptian subjects of the Ptolemaic Kingdom.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ptolemaios I memopulerkannya untuk menyatukan rakyat Yunani dan Mesir.",
        "en": "Ptolemy I popularizes him to unite Greek and Egyptian subjects."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c03",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "The cultus of Serapis was spread as a matter of deliberate policy by subsequent Ptolemaic kings.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raja Ptolemaios berikutnya menyebarkan kultus itu sebagai kebijakan.",
        "en": "Later Ptolemaic kings spread the cult as policy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c04",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "Serapis continued to increase in popularity during the Roman Empire, often replacing Osiris as the consort of Isis in temples outside Egypt.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada masa Romawi ia sering menggantikan Osiris sebagai pasangan Isis di luar Mesir.",
        "en": "In Roman times he often replaces Osiris as Isis’s consort outside Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c05",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "Originally known in Demotic as wsjr-ḥp, (\"Osiris-Apis\"), the name of the deity is derived from the syncretic worship of Osiris and the bull Apis as a single deity under the Egyptian name wsjr-ḥp. This name was later written in Coptic as ⲟⲩⲥⲉⲣϩⲁⲡⲓ Userhapi; Greeks sometimes used an uncommon form Sorapis (Koine Greek: Σόραπις), slightly closer to the Egyptian name(s).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berasal dari pemujaan gabungan Osiris-Apis.",
        "en": "His name derives from the combined worship of Osiris-Apis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c06",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "Sarapis (Σάραπις, earlier form) was the most common form in Ancient Greek until Roman times, when Serapis (Koine Greek: Σέραπις, later form) became common.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarapis merupakan bentuk Yunani awal, sedangkan Serapis menjadi umum pada masa Romawi.",
        "en": "Sarapis is the earlier Greek form, while Serapis becomes common in Roman times."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c07",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "A serapeum (Koine Greek: σεραπεῖον serapeion) was any temple or religious precinct devoted to Serapis. The most renowned serapeum was in Alexandria.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Serapeum adalah kuil atau kawasan ibadah untuk Serapis.",
        "en": "A serapeum is a temple or religious precinct dedicated to Serapis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c08",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "The most renowned serapeum was in Alexandria.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Serapeum paling terkenal berada di Alexandria.",
        "en": "The best-known serapeum is at Alexandria."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c09",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "Serapis was depicted as a Greek god in general appearance with Egyptian trappings, sometimes identified either as Pluto (Hades), Osiris, Dionysus, Amun, Zeus, Jupiter, Pan, Asclepius, and Dis Pater. The depictions of Serapis combined iconography from many Egyptian Greek cults, in most cases signifying the abundance and resurrection, namely, as the cornucopia horn and Calathus basket.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambarannya memadukan penampilan dewa Yunani dengan atribut Mesir.",
        "en": "His depictions combine Greek divine appearance with Egyptian attributes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c10",
      "source_id": "serapis-s1",
      "quote": "was shown enthroned with the modius, a basket/grain-measure, on his head ... with Cerberus, gatekeeper of the underworld, resting at his feet.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patung kultus Alexandria menunjukkan figur bertahta dengan ukuran biji-bijian di kepala dan Cerberus di kaki.",
        "en": "The Alexandrian cult statue shows an enthroned figure with a grain measure on his head and Cerberus at his feet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c11",
      "source_id": "serapis-s2",
      "quote": "the Greek gods Zeus, Helios, Dionysius, Hades, and Asklepius.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 172",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber ini menghubungkan sifat Serapis dengan Zeus, Helios, Dionysos, Hades, dan Asklepios.",
        "en": "This source connects Serapis’s attributes with Zeus, Helios, Dionysus, Hades and Asclepius."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c12",
      "source_id": "serapis-s2",
      "quote": "He was the supreme deity worshipped at the famous Serapeum nearby the Library of Alexandria.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 172",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi dewa tertinggi yang dipuja di Serapeum dekat Perpustakaan Alexandria.",
        "en": "He becomes the supreme deity worshipped at the Serapeum near Alexandria’s Library."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serapis-c13",
      "source_id": "serapis-s2",

    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tefnut adalah dewi kelembapan Mesir dan anggota Ennead Heliopolis. Atum disebut sebagai orang tuanya.",
      "en": "Tefnut is an Egyptian moisture goddess belonging to the Heliopolitan Ennead. Atum is named as her parent.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tefnut-c01",
        "tefnut-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Shu adalah saudara laki-laki sekaligus pasangannya. Geb dan Nut adalah anak-anaknya bersama Shu. Ia berupa singa betina atau dewi berkepala singa. Ia membantu Shu menopang langit dan menjaga kestabilan dunia. Dalam satu kisah ia pergi dari Mesir ke gurun Nubia sebagai singa betina ganas. Ia dapat diidentifikasi dengan Bastet, Sekhmet, dan Hathor. Sumber ini menggambarkan Tefnut sebagai dewi atmosfer dekat bumi. Namanya dikaitkan dengan bunyi meludah dalam penafsiran yang dikutip sumber. Seni juga menggambarkannya sebagai ular berkepala singa. Satu versi penciptaan melahirkan Shu dan Tefnut lewat ludah Atum. Budge menghubungkan Tefnut sebagai dewi orang mati dengan minuman bagi almarhum. Dalam uraian Budge, Tefnut adalah mata kiri Temu, dibandingkan dengan bulan. Temu, Shu, dan Tefnut membentuk tiga serangkai dalam teks penciptaan yang dikutip Budge.",
      "en": "Shu is her brother and consort. Geb and Nut are her children with Shu. She takes the form of a lioness or lion-headed goddess. She helps Shu support the sky and maintain the world’s stability. In one tale she leaves Egypt for the Nubian desert as a fierce lioness. She can be identified with Bastet, Sekhmet and Hathor. This source describes Tefnut as goddess of the atmosphere near the earth. Her name is associated with the sound of spitting in the interpretation cited by this source. Art also depicts her as a lion-headed serpent. One creation version produces Shu and Tefnut through Atum’s spitting. Budge connects Tefnut as a goddess of the dead with drink for the deceased. In Budge’s account Tefnut is Temu’s left eye, compared with the moon. Temu, Shu and Tefnut form a trinity in the creation text cited by Budge.",

      "quote": "テフヌト（Tefnut）は、エジプト神話における湿気の女神。ヘリオポリス九柱神に数えられる。テフヌト、テフヌウト、テフェネト、テフヌートなどとも表記され、ギリシア語ではトフェニスと呼ばれる。",

      "id": "tefnut-c02",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s1",
      "quote": "創造神アトゥムを親に持ち、アトゥムの自慰によって生まれた（ラーと習合されたため、ラーともされる）。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Atum disebut sebagai orang tuanya.",
        "en": "Atum is named as her parent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c03",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s1",
      "quote": "配偶神は兄でもある大気の神シュー。彼との間に大地の神ゲブと天空の女神ヌトを成した。雌ライオンもしくは",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shu adalah saudara laki-laki sekaligus pasangannya.",
        "en": "Shu is her brother and consort."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c04",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s1",
      "quote": "配偶神は兄でもある大気の神シュー。彼との間に大地の神ゲブと天空の女神ヌトを成した。雌ライオンもしくは",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Geb dan Nut adalah anak-anaknya bersama Shu.",
        "en": "Geb and Nut are her children with Shu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c05",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s1",
      "quote": "雌ライオンもしくは、ライオンの頭を持った女神として描かれる。シューの妻としての伝承ばかりでテフヌト単独での伝承は、ほとんど見られない。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berupa singa betina atau dewi berkepala singa.",
        "en": "She takes the form of a lioness or lion-headed goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c06",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s1",
      "quote": "テフヌトは天空を押し上げる夫を助け、一心同体の存在として世界の安定のために働く。 他の神々との習合",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membantu Shu menopang langit dan menjaga kestabilan dunia.",
        "en": "She helps Shu support the sky and maintain the world’s stability."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c07",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s1",
      "quote": "テフヌトは、シューの妻となる前に家出をしたという神話があり、宮殿で退屈していたテフヌトは父親（ここでは太陽神ラーとされる）に何も告げず、南のヌビア砂漠へ家出してしまう。獰猛な雌ライオンとなって気ままに駆け回っていたテフヌトに父親は、息子シューと知恵の神トートに依頼し、ようやく連れ戻すことに成功した、という内容である。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu kisah ia pergi dari Mesir ke gurun Nubia sebagai singa betina ganas.",
        "en": "In one tale she leaves Egypt for the Nubian desert as a fierce lioness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c08",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s1",
      "quote": "太陽神ラーの娘であること、雌ライオンの姿を持つことから、エジプトの女神バステトとセクメト、セクメトと同一視された女神ハトホルと同一視されることがある。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dapat diidentifikasi dengan Bastet, Sekhmet, dan Hathor.",
        "en": "She can be identified with Bastet, Sekhmet and Hathor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c09",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s2",
      "quote": "She is goddess of the atmosphere of the lower world, the earth, just as Shu is god of the upper atmosphere above the earth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 199",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber ini menggambarkan Tefnut sebagai dewi atmosfer dekat bumi.",
        "en": "This source describes Tefnut as goddess of the atmosphere near the earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c10",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s2",
      "quote": "her name represents the sound of spitting",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 199",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dikaitkan dengan bunyi meludah dalam penafsiran yang dikutip sumber.",
        "en": "Her name is associated with the sound of spitting in the interpretation cited by this source."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c11",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s2",
      "quote": "She is most often depicted as a woman seated with a lion's head or a serpent with a lion's head.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 199",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seni juga menggambarkannya sebagai ular berkepala singa.",
        "en": "Art also depicts her as a lion-headed serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c12",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s2",
      "quote": "Shu and Tefnut were the first two gods Atum created either by mating with his shadow or by spitting.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 199",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu versi penciptaan melahirkan Shu dan Tefnut lewat ludah Atum.",
        "en": "One creation version produces Shu and Tefnut through Atum’s spitting."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c13",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s3",
      "quote": "as a god of the dead she seems to have been, in some way, connected with the supply of drink to the deceased.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 121",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge menghubungkan Tefnut sebagai dewi orang mati dengan minuman bagi almarhum.",
        "en": "Budge connects Tefnut as a goddess of the dead with drink for the deceased."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c14",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s3",
      "quote": "she was the left, i.e., Shu represented an aspect of the Sun, and Tefnut of the Moon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 121",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam uraian Budge, Tefnut adalah mata kiri Temu, dibandingkan dengan bulan.",
        "en": "In Budge’s account Tefnut is Temu’s left eye, compared with the moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tefnut-c15",
      "source_id": "tefnut-s3",
      "quote": "The gods Temu, Shu, and Tefnut thus formed a trinity",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 121",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Temu, Shu, dan Tefnut membentuk tiga serangkai dalam teks penciptaan yang dikutip Budge.",
        "en": "Temu, Shu and Tefnut form a trinity in the creation text cited by Budge."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Ilustrasi dalam artikel tidak dipakai tanpa pemeriksaan berkas Commons, kecocokan subjek, dan lisensinya; belum ada gambar yang terverifikasi untuk entri ini."

    }
  ]
}

  "countries": null,

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ammit adalah makhluk Mesir dalam pengadilan orang mati, berwujud gabungan singa, kuda nil, dan buaya.",
    "en": "Ammit is an Egyptian being of the judgment of the dead, combining lion, hippopotamus and crocodile features.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ammit-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ammit adalah makhluk Mesir dalam pengadilan orang mati, berwujud gabungan singa, kuda nil, dan buaya. Namanya berarti pelahap atau penelan orang mati.",
      "en": "Ammit is an Egyptian being of the judgment of the dead, combining lion, hippopotamus and crocodile features. Her name means devourer or swallower of the dead.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ammit-c01",
        "ammit-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tubuh gabungannya menggambarkan ancaman pemusnahan yang tidak dapat dihindari. Ia mulai muncul dalam versi Kitab Orang Mati pada Kerajaan Baru. Ammit ditakuti alih-alih menjadi pusat pemujaan. Ammit menelan hati jiwa yang gagal memperoleh pembenaran Osiris.",
      "en": "Her composite body conveys an inescapable threat of annihilation. She begins appearing in versions of the Book of the Dead in the New Kingdom. Ammit is feared rather than a focus of worship. Ammit devours hearts of souls not justified by Osiris.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ammit-c03",
        "ammit-c04",
        "ammit-c05",
        "ammit-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "ammit-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ammit",
      "title": "Ammit",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "ammit-s2",
      "url": "https://www.worldhistory.org/article/885/egyptian-gods---the-complete-list/",
      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ammit-c01",
      "source_id": "ammit-s1",
      "quote": "was an ancient Egyptian goddess with the forequarters of a lion, the hindquarters of a hippopotamus, and the head of a crocodile",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ammit adalah makhluk Mesir dalam pengadilan orang mati, berwujud gabungan singa, kuda nil, dan buaya.",
        "en": "Ammit is an Egyptian being of the judgment of the dead, combining lion, hippopotamus and crocodile features."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ammit-c02",
      "source_id": "ammit-s1",
      "quote": "means \"devourer of the dead\" (\"devoureress of the dead\") or \"swallower of the dead\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berarti pelahap atau penelan orang mati.",
        "en": "Her name means devourer or swallower of the dead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ammit-c03",
      "source_id": "ammit-s1",
      "quote": "The combination of three deadly animals of the Nile suggests that no one can escape annihilation, even in the afterlife.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tubuh gabungannya menggambarkan ancaman pemusnahan yang tidak dapat dihindari.",
        "en": "Her composite body conveys an inescapable threat of annihilation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ammit-c04",
      "source_id": "ammit-s1",
      "quote": "Versions of the Book of the Dead from the New Kingdom started to include Ammit. During the eighteenth dynasty, the crocodile-lion-hippopotamus hybrid was the conventional depiction of Ammit. She appeared in scenes showing the Judgment of the Dead, in tombs and funerary papyri.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mulai muncul dalam versi Kitab Orang Mati pada Kerajaan Baru.",
        "en": "She begins appearing in versions of the Book of the Dead in the New Kingdom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ammit-c05",
      "source_id": "ammit-s1",
      "quote": "Unlike other gods featured in ancient Egyptian religion, Ammit was not worshipped. Instead, Ammit was feared and believed to be a demon rather than a deity, due to her role as the 'devourer of the dead'. During the New Kingdom, deities and demons were differentiated by having a cult or center of worship.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ammit ditakuti alih-alih menjadi pusat pemujaan.",
        "en": "Ammit is feared rather than a focus of worship."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ammit-c06",
      "source_id": "ammit-s2",
      "quote": "devoured the hearts of those souls which were not justified by Osiris.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 20",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ammit menelan hati jiwa yang gagal memperoleh pembenaran Osiris.",
        "en": "Ammit devours hearts of souls not justified by Osiris."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Serket adalah dewi Mesir pelindung terhadap sengatan dan gigitan kalajengking.",
    "en": "Serket is an Egyptian goddess protecting against scorpion stings and bites.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "serket-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Serket adalah dewi Mesir pelindung terhadap sengatan dan gigitan kalajengking. Ia digambarkan dengan kalajengking di kepala, ankh, dan tongkat was.",
      "en": "Serket is an Egyptian goddess protecting against scorpion stings and bites. She is shown with a scorpion on her head, an ankh and a was-sceptre.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "serket-c01",
        "serket-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia berhubungan dengan penyembuhan, sihir, dan perlindungan. Ia melindungi tempat pembalseman dan Qebehsenuef penjaga bejana kanopik. Kitab Dua Jalan menempatkannya sebagai penjaga belokan jalan dunia bawah. Patung emas Serket ditemukan dalam makam Tutankhamun.",
      "en": "She is associated with healing, magic and protection. She protects embalming tents and Qebehsenuef, a guardian of canopic jars. The Book of Two Ways makes her guardian of a bend in the underworld path. A golden statue of Serket is found in Tutankhamun’s tomb.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "serket-c03",
        "serket-c04",
        "serket-c05",
        "serket-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "serket-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serket",
      "title": "Serket",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "serket-s2",
      "url": "https://www.worldhistory.org/article/885/egyptian-gods---the-complete-list/",
      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "serket-c01",
      "source_id": "serket-s1",
      "quote": "is the goddess of protection against the venomous stings and bites of scorpions in Egyptian mythology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Serket adalah dewi Mesir pelindung terhadap sengatan dan gigitan kalajengking.",
        "en": "Serket is an Egyptian goddess protecting against scorpion stings and bites."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serket-c02",
      "source_id": "serket-s1",
      "quote": "Serket is often depicted as a woman whose head is surmounted by a scorpion ... an ankh in one hand, and a was-sceptre in her other.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan dengan kalajengking di kepala, ankh, dan tongkat was.",
        "en": "She is shown with a scorpion on her head, an ankh and a was-sceptre."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serket-c03",
      "source_id": "serket-s1",
      "quote": "She is associated with healing, magic, and protection. Another interpretation of her name is, 'she who gives breath.' As many of the venomous creatures of Egypt could prove fatal, Serket was considered a protector of the dead, particularly being associated with venoms and fluids that cause stiffening.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berhubungan dengan penyembuhan, sihir, dan perlindungan.",
        "en": "She is associated with healing, magic and protection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serket-c04",
      "source_id": "serket-s1",
      "quote": "During the Old Kingdom she held a protective role around the throne of the king. However, her primary role was with the funerary cult. She was said to be the protector of the tents of embalmers and is one of the tutelary goddesses depicted on the canopic chest which contains four canopic jars.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melindungi tempat pembalseman dan Qebehsenuef penjaga bejana kanopik.",
        "en": "She protects embalming tents and Qebehsenuef, a guardian of canopic jars."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serket-c05",
      "source_id": "serket-s1",
      "quote": "During the Middle Kingdom, Egyptian myth says that Serket's help is required in the Underworld where according to the Book of the Two Ways she guards a twist in the pathway. She is also given credit for binding and containing the snake deity, Apophis (or Apep).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab Dua Jalan menempatkannya sebagai penjaga belokan jalan dunia bawah.",
        "en": "The Book of Two Ways makes her guardian of a bend in the underworld path."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "serket-c06",
      "source_id": "serket-s2",
      "quote": "She is best known from her golden statue found in the tomb of Tutankhamun.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 174",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patung emas Serket ditemukan dalam makam Tutankhamun.",
        "en": "A golden statue of Serket is found in Tutankhamun’s tomb."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Taweret adalah dewi Mesir pelindung kelahiran dan kesuburan.",
    "en": "Taweret is an Egyptian goddess protecting childbirth and fertility.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "taweret-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Taweret adalah dewi Mesir pelindung kelahiran dan kesuburan. Namanya berarti Yang Agung.",
      "en": "Taweret is an Egyptian goddess protecting childbirth and fertility. Her name means the Great One.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taweret-c01",
        "taweret-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia berupa kuda nil betina berkaki dua dengan unsur singa dan buaya. Ia dekat dengan dewi kuda nil Ipet, Reret, dan Hedjet. Gambarnya menghiasi pisau atau tongkat magis untuk ritual perlindungan bayi. Taweret juga disebut pasangan Bes dalam sumber pendamping.",
      "en": "She takes the form of a bipedal female hippopotamus with lion and crocodile features. She is closely associated with the hippopotamus goddesses Ipet, Reret and Hedjet. Her image adorns magical knives or wands used in infant-protection rites. Taweret is also called Bes’s consort in the accompanying source.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taweret-c03",
        "taweret-c04",
        "taweret-c05",
        "taweret-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "taweret-s1",

    {
      "id": "taweret-s2",

      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,

      "published": null,
      "language": "en",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "taweret-c01",
      "source_id": "taweret-s1",
      "quote": "is the protective goddess of childbirth and fertility.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taweret adalah dewi Mesir pelindung kelahiran dan kesuburan.",
        "en": "Taweret is an Egyptian goddess protecting childbirth and fertility."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taweret-c02",
      "source_id": "taweret-s1",
      "quote": "The name \"Taweret\" means \"she who is great\" or simply \"great one\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berarti Yang Agung.",
        "en": "Her name means the Great One."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taweret-c03",
      "source_id": "taweret-s1",
      "quote": "The deity is typically depicted as a bipedal female hippopotamus with feline attributes, pendulous female human breasts, the limbs and paws of a lion, and the back and tail of a Nile crocodile.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berupa kuda nil betina berkaki dua dengan unsur singa dan buaya.",
        "en": "She takes the form of a bipedal female hippopotamus with lion and crocodile features."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taweret-c04",
      "source_id": "taweret-s1",
      "quote": "From her ideological conception, Taweret was closely grouped with (and is often indistinguishable from) several other protective hippopotamus goddesses: Ipet, Reret, and Hedjet. Some scholars even interpret these goddesses as aspects of the same deity, considering their universally shared role as protective household goddesses.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dekat dengan dewi kuda nil Ipet, Reret, dan Hedjet.",
        "en": "She is closely associated with the hippopotamus goddesses Ipet, Reret and Hedjet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taweret-c05",
      "source_id": "taweret-s1",
      "quote": "It was not until the Middle Kingdom of Egypt (c. 2055–1650 BCE) that Taweret became featured more prominently as a figure of religious devotion. Her image adorns magical objects, the most notable of which being a common type of \"wand\" or \"knife\" carved from hippopotamus ivory that was likely used in rituals associated with birth and the protection of infants.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambarnya menghiasi pisau atau tongkat magis untuk ritual perlindungan bayi.",
        "en": "Her image adorns magical knives or wands used in infant-protection rites."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taweret-c06",
      "source_id": "taweret-s2",
      "quote": "She is further identified as the consort of Bes, the dwarf god of childbirth, sexuality, humor, and war.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 197",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taweret juga disebut pasangan Bes dalam sumber pendamping.",
        "en": "Taweret is also called Bes’s consort in the accompanying source."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nun adalah personifikasi jurang air primordial dalam agama Mesir, tempat Ra muncul.",
    "en": "Nun personifies the primordial watery abyss in Egyptian religion, from which Ra arises.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "nun-q276563-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nun adalah personifikasi jurang air primordial dalam agama Mesir, tempat Ra muncul. Ia anggota Ogdoad yang mewakili kekacauan sebelum penciptaan.",
      "en": "Nun personifies the primordial watery abyss in Egyptian religion, from which Ra arises. He is an Ogdoad member representing chaos before creation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nun-q276563-c01",
        "nun-q276563-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia juga dipandang mengembalikan keberadaan ke keadaan primordial. Naunet adalah pasangan atau aspek femininnya. Bukit pertama dunia muncul dari air Nun. Budge menyebut Nu sebagai ayah para dewa dan sumber air primordial semua hal.",
      "en": "He is also seen as returning existence to its primordial condition. Naunet is his consort or feminine aspect. The world’s first mound emerges from Nun’s waters. Budge calls Nu father of the gods and primordial watery source of all things.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nun-q276563-c03",
        "nun-q276563-c04",
        "nun-q276563-c05",
        "nun-q276563-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "nun-q276563-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nun_(mythology)",
      "title": "Nun (mythology)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "nun-q276563-s2",
      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/11277/pg11277-images.html",
      "title": "Egyptian Ideas of the Future Life",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Project Gutenberg",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nun-q276563-c01",
      "source_id": "nun-q276563-s1",
      "quote": "in ancient Egyptian religion, is the personification of the primordial watery abyss which existed at the time of creation and from which the creator sun god Ra arose.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nun adalah personifikasi jurang air primordial dalam agama Mesir, tempat Ra muncul.",
        "en": "Nun personifies the primordial watery abyss in Egyptian religion, from which Ra arises."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nun-q276563-c02",
      "source_id": "nun-q276563-s1",
      "quote": "Nun is one of the eight deities of the Ogdoad representing ancient Egyptian primordial Chaos from which the primordial mound arose. Nun can be seen as the first of all the gods and the creator of reality and personification of the cosmos. Nun is also considered the god that will destroy existence and return everything to the Nun whence it came. No cult was addressed to Nun.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia anggota Ogdoad yang mewakili kekacauan sebelum penciptaan.",
        "en": "He is an Ogdoad member representing chaos before creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nun-q276563-c03",
      "source_id": "nun-q276563-s1",
      "quote": "Nun is also considered the god that will destroy existence and return everything to the Nun whence it came.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga dipandang mengembalikan keberadaan ke keadaan primordial.",
        "en": "He is also seen as returning existence to its primordial condition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nun-q276563-c04",
      "source_id": "nun-q276563-s1",
      "quote": "Nun's consort (or his female aspect) was the goddess Nunut or Naunet (Ancient Egyptian: 𓏌𓐱𓏌𓐰𓏌𓐰𓇯𓈗𓏏𓐰𓆇𓁐, romanized: nnwt).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naunet adalah pasangan atau aspek femininnya.",
        "en": "Naunet is his consort or feminine aspect."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nun-q276563-c05",
      "source_id": "nun-q276563-s1",
      "quote": "In ancient Egyptian creation accounts, the original mound of land comes forth from the waters of the Nun.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bukit pertama dunia muncul dari air Nun.",
        "en": "The world’s first mound emerges from Nun’s waters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nun-q276563-c06",
      "source_id": "nun-q276563-s2",
      "quote": "NU was the \"father of the gods,\" and progenitor of the \"great company\r\n  of the gods\"; he was the primeval watery mass out of which all things\r\n  came.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 129",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Budge menyebut Nu sebagai ayah para dewa dan sumber air primordial semua hal.",
        "en": "Budge calls Nu father of the gods and primordial watery source of all things."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "en": "Wadjet is an Egyptian goddess originally worshipped at Dep or Buto in Lower Egypt.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wadjet-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Wadjet adalah dewi Mesir yang semula dipuja di Dep atau Buto, Mesir Hilir. Ia terkait lambang Mata Ra dan Mata Horus.",
      "en": "Wadjet is an Egyptian goddess originally worshipped at Dep or Buto in Lower Egypt. She is connected with the Eye of Ra and Eye of Horus symbols.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wadjet-c01",
        "wadjet-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya berasal dari istilah papirus sebagai lambang Mesir Hilir. Bersama Nekhbet ia menjadi pelindung seluruh Mesir. Satu mitos menjadikannya pengasuh Horus yang membantu Isis melindunginya. Sumber ini mengisahkan Wadjet menanam papirus pertama dan membantu Isis membesarkan Horus.",
      "en": "Her name derives from the term for papyrus as a symbol of Lower Egypt. With Nekhbet she becomes a protector of all Egypt. One myth makes her Horus’s nurse, helping Isis protect him. This source tells of Wadjet planting the first papyrus and helping Isis raise Horus.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wadjet-c03",
        "wadjet-c04",
        "wadjet-c05",
        "wadjet-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "stories": [],
  "places": [],

  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wadjet-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadjet",
      "title": "Wadjet",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "wadjet-s2",
      "url": "https://www.worldhistory.org/article/885/egyptian-gods---the-complete-list/",
      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wadjet-c01",
      "source_id": "wadjet-s1",
      "quote": "was originally the ancient Egyptian local goddess of the city of Dep or Buto in Lower Egypt",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wadjet adalah dewi Mesir yang semula dipuja di Dep atau Buto, Mesir Hilir.",
        "en": "Wadjet is an Egyptian goddess originally worshipped at Dep or Buto in Lower Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wadjet-c02",
      "source_id": "wadjet-s1",
      "quote": "Wadjet was closely associated in ancient Egyptian religion with the Eye of Ra and the Eye of Horus symbols, each powerful protective deities. The hieroglyph for her eye is shown below; sometimes two are shown in the sky of religious images. There is little consensus on which eye is truly tied to Wadjet as both have some importance to her.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia terkait lambang Mata Ra dan Mata Horus.",
        "en": "She is connected with the Eye of Ra and Eye of Horus symbols."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wadjet-c03",
      "source_id": "wadjet-s1",
      "quote": "The name Wadjet is derived from the term for the symbol of her domain, Lower Egypt, the papyrus. Its hieroglyphs differ from those of the Green Crown or Deshret of Lower Egypt only by the determinative, which in the case of the crown was a picture of the Green Crown and, in the case of the goddess, a rearing cobra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berasal dari istilah papirus sebagai lambang Mesir Hilir.",
        "en": "Her name derives from the term for papyrus as a symbol of Lower Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wadjet-c04",
      "source_id": "wadjet-s1",
      "quote": "Wadjet was said to be the matron and protector of Lower Egypt in her cobra form, and upon unification with her sister Nekhbet, Goddess of Upper Egypt, the joint protector and patron of all of Egypt. Scenes of the two sisters together as two cobras can be found in King Sety I's tomb, located in the Valley of the Kings.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bersama Nekhbet ia menjadi pelindung seluruh Mesir.",
        "en": "With Nekhbet she becomes a protector of all Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wadjet-c05",
      "source_id": "wadjet-s1",
      "quote": "In another myth, Wadjet was said to be the nurse of the infant god Horus. With the help of his mother Isis, they protected Horus from his treacherous uncle, Set, when they took refuge in the swamps of the Nile Delta.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu mitos menjadikannya pengasuh Horus yang membantu Isis melindunginya.",
        "en": "One myth makes her Horus’s nurse, helping Isis protect him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wadjet-c06",
      "source_id": "wadjet-s2",
      "quote": "She planted the first papyrus plants, laid out the papyrus fields in the swamps of the Nile Delta, and helped Isis raise Horus there",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 210",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber ini mengisahkan Wadjet menanam papirus pertama dan membantu Isis membesarkan Horus.",
        "en": "This source tells of Wadjet planting the first papyrus and helping Isis raise Horus."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Nekhbet is an Egyptian goddess protecting Nekheb and later Upper Egypt.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nekhbet adalah dewi Mesir pelindung Nekheb yang kemudian melindungi Mesir Hulu. Kuil awalnya berada di Nekheb atau El Kab.",
      "en": "Nekhbet is an Egyptian goddess protecting Nekheb and later Upper Egypt. Her early shrine is at Nekheb or El Kab.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nekhbet-c01",
        "nekhbet-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dan Wadjet tampil sebagai Dua Nyonya dalam gelar kerajaan. Ia digambarkan sebagai burung nasar. Ia melayang dengan sayap terentang dan simbol shen sebagai perlindungan kekal. Sumber pendamping menegaskan pasangan Nekhbet dan Wadjet sebagai Dua Nyonya.",
      "en": "She and Wadjet appear as the Two Ladies in royal titles. She is depicted as a vulture. She hovers with spread wings and a shen symbol for eternal protection. The accompanying source confirms Nekhbet and Wadjet as the Two Ladies.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nekhbet-c03",
        "nekhbet-c04",
        "nekhbet-c05",
        "nekhbet-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nekhbet-c01",
      "source_id": "nekhbet-s1",
      "quote": "is an early predynastic local goddess in Egyptian mythology, who was the patron of the city of Nekheb",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nekhbet adalah dewi Mesir pelindung Nekheb yang kemudian melindungi Mesir Hulu.",
        "en": "Nekhbet is an Egyptian goddess protecting Nekheb and later Upper Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nekhbet-c02",
      "source_id": "nekhbet-s1",
      "quote": "One of Egypt's earliest temples was the shrine of Nekhbet at Nekheb (also referred to as El Kab). It was the companion city to Nekhen, the religious and political capital of Upper Egypt, at the end of the Predynastic period (c. 3200–3100 BC) and probably, also during the Early Dynastic Period (c. 3100–2686 BC).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuil awalnya berada di Nekheb atau El Kab.",
        "en": "Her early shrine is at Nekheb or El Kab."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nekhbet-c03",
      "source_id": "nekhbet-s1",
      "quote": "Nekhbet was the tutelary deity of Upper Egypt. Nekhbet and her Lower Egyptian counterpart Wadjet often appeared together as the \"Two Ladies\". One of the titles of each ruler was the Nebty name, which began with the hieroglyphs for [s/he] of the Two Ladies....",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dan Wadjet tampil sebagai Dua Nyonya dalam gelar kerajaan.",
        "en": "She and Wadjet appear as the Two Ladies in royal titles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nekhbet-c04",
      "source_id": "nekhbet-s1",
      "quote": "In art, Nekhbet was depicted as a vulture.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai burung nasar.",
        "en": "She is depicted as a vulture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nekhbet-c05",
      "source_id": "nekhbet-s1",
      "quote": "Nekhbet usually was depicted hovering, with her wings spread above the royal image, clutching a shen symbol (representing eternal encircling protection), frequently in her claws.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melayang dengan sayap terentang dan simbol shen sebagai perlindungan kekal.",
        "en": "She hovers with spread wings and a shen symbol for eternal protection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nekhbet-c06",
      "source_id": "nekhbet-s2",
      "quote": "Nekhbet - A protector goddess in the form of a vulture who guarded Upper Egypt. She was associated with Wadjet, protector of Lower Egypt. The two are referred to as \"The Two Ladies\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 136",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber pendamping menegaskan pasangan Nekhbet dan Wadjet sebagai Dua Nyonya.",
        "en": "The accompanying source confirms Nekhbet and Wadjet as the Two Ladies."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Ennead Agung adalah kelompok sembilan dewa Mesir yang dipuja di Heliopolis. Anggotanya mencakup Atum, Shu, Tefnut, Geb, Nut, Osiris, Isis, Set, dan Nephthys.",
      "en": "The Great Ennead is a group of nine Egyptian gods worshipped at Heliopolis. Its members include Atum, Shu, Tefnut, Geb, Nut, Osiris, Isis, Set and Nephthys.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ennead-c01",
        "ennead-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ennead Heliopolis merupakan salah satu dari beberapa kelompok sembilan dewa. Kata ennead berasal melalui bahasa Latin dari bahasa Yunani yang berarti sembilan. Teks Piramida menyebut Ennead Agung, Kecil, Ganda, dan Tujuh Ennead. Ennead mengadili sengketa Set dan Horus mengenai takhta.",
      "en": "Heliopolis’s Ennead is one of several groups of nine gods. The word ennead comes through Latin from Greek meaning nine. The Pyramid Texts mention the Great, Lesser, Dual and Seven Enneads. The Ennead judges Set and Horus’s dispute over the throne.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ennead-c01",
      "source_id": "ennead-s1",
      "quote": "The Ennead or Great Ennead was a group of nine deities in Egyptian mythology worshipped at Heliopolis: the sun god Atum; his children Shu and Tefnut; their children Geb and Nut; and their children Osiris, Isis, Set, and Nephthys. The Ennead sometimes includes Horus the Elder; an ancient form of the falcon god, not the son of Osiris and Isis.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ennead Agung adalah kelompok sembilan dewa Mesir yang dipuja di Heliopolis.",
        "en": "The Great Ennead is a group of nine Egyptian gods worshipped at Heliopolis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ennead-c02",
      "source_id": "ennead-s1",
      "quote": "the sun god Atum; his children Shu and Tefnut; their children Geb and Nut; and their children Osiris, Isis, Set, and Nephthys.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anggotanya mencakup Atum, Shu, Tefnut, Geb, Nut, Osiris, Isis, Set, dan Nephthys.",
        "en": "Its members include Atum, Shu, Tefnut, Geb, Nut, Osiris, Isis, Set and Nephthys."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ennead-c03",
      "source_id": "ennead-s1",
      "quote": "The Great Ennead was only one of several such groupings of nine deities in ancient Egypt.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ennead Heliopolis merupakan salah satu dari beberapa kelompok sembilan dewa.",
        "en": "Heliopolis’s Ennead is one of several groups of nine gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ennead-c04",
      "source_id": "ennead-s1",
      "quote": "The English name ennead is a borrowing via Latin of the Greek name enneás (ἐννεάς), meaning \"the nine\". The term was a calque of the Egyptian name, written psḏt and also meaning \"the Nine\". Its original pronunciation is uncertain, since hieroglyphs do not record vowels, but may have been /piˈsiːcʼat/ in Old Egyptian, /piˈsiːtʼaʔ/ in Middle Egyptian, and /pəˈsiːtʼə/ in Late Egyptian.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ennead berasal melalui bahasa Latin dari bahasa Yunani yang berarti sembilan.",
        "en": "The word ennead comes through Latin from Greek meaning nine."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ennead-c05",
      "source_id": "ennead-s1",
      "quote": "The Pyramid Texts of Dynasties V and VI mention the \"Great Ennead\", the \"Lesser Ennead\", the \"Dual Ennead\", and the \"Seven Enneads\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Piramida menyebut Ennead Agung, Kecil, Ganda, dan Tujuh Ennead.",
        "en": "The Pyramid Texts mention the Great, Lesser, Dual and Seven Enneads."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ennead-c06",
      "source_id": "ennead-s2",
      "quote": "These nine gods decide whether Set or Horus should rule in the story The Contendings of Horus and Set.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 59",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ennead mengadili sengketa Set dan Horus mengenai takhta.",
        "en": "The Ennead judges Set and Horus’s dispute over the throne."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Monthu atau Montu adalah dewa perang elang Mesir, terutama dipuja di Mesir Hulu dan Thebes.",
    "en": "Monthu or Montu is an Egyptian falcon war god, especially worshipped in Upper Egypt and Thebes.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "monthu-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Monthu atau Montu adalah dewa perang elang Mesir, terutama dipuja di Mesir Hulu dan Thebes. Namanya ditranskripsikan mntw dan dikaitkan dengan makna pengembara.",
      "en": "Monthu or Montu is an Egyptian falcon war god, especially worshipped in Upper Egypt and Thebes. His name is transcribed mntw and associated with the meaning nomad.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "monthu-c01",
        "monthu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia terkait Ra sebagai Montu-Ra dan menghadapi musuh Maat. Banteng suci Buchis menjadi manifestasinya. Seni menggambarkannya berkepala elang atau banteng dengan cakram matahari dan bulu. Orang Yunani menyetarakan Montu dengan Apollo.",
      "en": "He is linked with Ra as Montu-Ra and confronts enemies of Maat. The sacred bull Buchis becomes his manifestation. Art depicts him as falcon- or bull-headed with a solar disk and feathers. The Greeks equate Montu with Apollo.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "monthu-c03",
        "monthu-c04",
        "monthu-c05",
        "monthu-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "monthu-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montu",
      "title": "Montu",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,

      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "monthu-s2",
      "url": "https://www.worldhistory.org/article/885/egyptian-gods---the-complete-list/",
      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "monthu-c01",
      "source_id": "monthu-s1",
      "quote": "Montu was a falcon-war god in the ancient Egyptian religion, an embodiment of the conquering vitality of the pharaoh. He was particularly worshipped in Upper Egypt and in the district of Thebes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Monthu atau Montu adalah dewa perang elang Mesir, terutama dipuja di Mesir Hulu dan Thebes.",
        "en": "Monthu or Montu is an Egyptian falcon war god, especially worshipped in Upper Egypt and Thebes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monthu-c02",
      "source_id": "monthu-s1",
      "quote": "Montu's name, shown in Egyptian hieroglyphs to the right, is technically transcribed as mntw (meaning \"Nomad\"). Because of the difficulty in transcribing Egyptian vowels, it is often realized as Mont, Monthu, Montju, Ment or Menthu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya ditranskripsikan mntw dan dikaitkan dengan makna pengembara.",
        "en": "His name is transcribed mntw and associated with the meaning nomad."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monthu-c03",
      "source_id": "monthu-s1",
      "quote": "A very ancient god, Montu was originally a manifestation of the scorching effect of Ra, the sun – and as such often appeared under the epithet Montu-Ra. The destructiveness of this characteristic led to him gaining characteristics of a warrior, and eventually becoming a widely revered war-god.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia terkait Ra sebagai Montu-Ra dan menghadapi musuh Maat.",
        "en": "He is linked with Ra as Montu-Ra and confronts enemies of Maat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monthu-c04",
      "source_id": "monthu-s1",
      "quote": "Because of the association of raging bulls with strength and war, the Egyptians also believed that Montu manifested himself as a white, black-snouted bull named Buchis (hellenization of the original Bakha: a living bull revered in Armant) — to the point that, in the Late Period (7th-4th centuries BC), Montu was depicted with a bull's head too.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banteng suci Buchis menjadi manifestasinya.",
        "en": "The sacred bull Buchis becomes his manifestation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monthu-c05",
      "source_id": "monthu-s1",
      "quote": "In Egyptian art, Montu was depicted as a falcon-headed or bull-headed man, with his head surmounted by the solar disk (because of his conceptual link with Ra) with either a double or singular uraeus, and two feathers. The falcon was a symbol of the sky and the bull was a symbol of strength and war.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seni menggambarkannya berkepala elang atau banteng dengan cakram matahari dan bulu.",
        "en": "Art depicts him as falcon- or bull-headed with a solar disk and feathers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monthu-c06",
      "source_id": "monthu-s2",
      "quote": "The Greeks equated him with Apollo",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 129",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Yunani menyetarakan Montu dengan Apollo.",
        "en": "The Greeks equate Montu with Apollo."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Nefertem adalah dewa Mesir yang muncul sebagai teratai dari air primordial saat penciptaan. Ia mewakili sinar matahari pertama dan aroma teratai biru Mesir.",
      "en": "Nefertem is an Egyptian god emerging as a lotus from primordial waters at creation. He represents the first sunlight and the scent of the Egyptian blue lotus.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nefertem-c01",
        "nefertem-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ptah menjadi ayahnya, sedangkan Sekhmet atau Bastet dapat disebut ibunya. Ia sering berupa pemuda dengan bunga teratai biru di kepala. Patung kepala Tutankhamun muncul dari teratai merupakan penggambaran terkenal Nefertem. Nefertem dipanggil untuk penyembuhan melalui aroma dalam pengobatan Mesir.",
      "en": "Ptah becomes his father, while Sekhmet or Bastet may be called his mother. He is often a young man with blue lotus flowers around his head. A bust of Tutankhamun emerging from a lotus is a notable depiction of Nefertem. Nefertem is invoked for healing through aromas in Egyptian medicine.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nefertem-c03",
        "nefertem-c04",
        "nefertem-c05",
        "nefertem-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "statement": {
        "id": "Nefertem adalah dewa Mesir yang muncul sebagai teratai dari air primordial saat penciptaan.",
        "en": "Nefertem is an Egyptian god emerging as a lotus from primordial waters at creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nefertem-c02",
      "source_id": "nefertem-s1",
      "quote": "Nefertem represented both the first sunlight and the delightful smell of the Egyptian blue lotus flower",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mewakili sinar matahari pertama dan aroma teratai biru Mesir.",
        "en": "He represents the first sunlight and the scent of the Egyptian blue lotus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nefertem-c03",
      "source_id": "nefertem-s1",
      "quote": "Nefertem was eventually seen as the son of the creator god Ptah, and the goddesses Sekhmet and Bast were sometimes called his mother. In art, Nefertem is usually depicted as a beautiful young man having blue water-lily flowers around his head. As the son of Bastet, he also sometimes has the head of a lion or is a lion or cat reclining.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ptah menjadi ayahnya, sedangkan Sekhmet atau Bastet dapat disebut ibunya.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nefertem-c04",
      "source_id": "nefertem-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nefertem-c05",
      "source_id": "nefertem-s1",
      "quote": "One of the most notable depictions of Nefertem is the Head of Nefertem, a wooden bust depicting a young king Tutankhamun as Nefertem with his head emerging from a lotus flower.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",

    "en": "Heqet is an Egyptian fertility goddess depicted as a frog.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "heqet-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Heqet adalah dewi kesuburan Mesir yang digambarkan sebagai katak. Katak berkaitan dengan banjir tahunan Nil dan kesuburan.",
      "en": "Heqet is an Egyptian fertility goddess depicted as a frog. The frog is linked with the annual Nile flood and fertility.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heqet-c01",
        "heqet-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kultusnya diketahui setidaknya sejak masa Dinasti Awal. Ia dianggap sebagai istri Khnum yang membentuk tubuh anak dengan roda tembikar. Dalam mitos Osiris ia memberikan napas hidup kepada Horus saat lahir. Sumber pendamping menggambarkannya sebagai katak atau perempuan berkepala katak.",
      "en": "Her cult is known from at least the Early Dynastic Period. She is regarded as wife of Khnum, who shapes children’s bodies on a potter’s wheel. In the Osiris myth she breathes life into Horus at birth. The accompanying source depicts her as a frog or frog-headed woman.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heqet-c03",
        "heqet-c04",
        "heqet-c05",
        "heqet-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "heqet-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heqet",
      "title": "Heqet",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
