      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "heqet-s2",
      "url": "https://www.worldhistory.org/article/885/egyptian-gods---the-complete-list/",
      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "heqet-c01",
      "source_id": "heqet-s1",
      "quote": "Heqet (Egyptian ḥqt, also ḥqtyt \"Heqtit\"), sometimes spelled Heket, is an Egyptian goddess of fertility, identified with Hathor, represented in the form of a frog.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Heqet adalah dewi kesuburan Mesir yang digambarkan sebagai katak.",
        "en": "Heqet is an Egyptian fertility goddess depicted as a frog."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heqet-c02",
      "source_id": "heqet-s1",
      "quote": "To the Egyptians, the frog was an ancient symbol of fertility, related to the annual flooding of the Nile. \nHeqet was originally the female counterpart of Khnum, or the wife of Khnum, and eventually she also became the mother of Heru-ur.\nIt has been proposed that her name is the origin of the name of Hecate, the Greek goddess of witchcraft.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Katak berkaitan dengan banjir tahunan Nil dan kesuburan.",
        "en": "The frog is linked with the annual Nile flood and fertility."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heqet-c03",
      "source_id": "heqet-s1",
      "quote": "The beginning of her cult dates to the Early Dynastic Period at least. Her name was part of the names of some high-born Second Dynasty individuals buried at Helwan, such as prince Nisuheqet, and was mentioned on a stela of Wepemnofret and in the Pyramid Texts. Early frog statuettes are often thought to be depictions of her.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kultusnya diketahui setidaknya sejak masa Dinasti Awal.",
        "en": "Her cult is known from at least the Early Dynastic Period."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heqet-c04",
      "source_id": "heqet-s1",
      "quote": "Heqet was considered the wife of Khnum, who formed the bodies of new children on his potter's wheel.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap sebagai istri Khnum yang membentuk tubuh anak dengan roda tembikar.",
        "en": "She is regarded as wife of Khnum, who shapes children’s bodies on a potter’s wheel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heqet-c05",
      "source_id": "heqet-s1",
      "quote": "In the Osiris myth, it was Heqet who breathed life into the new body of Horus at birth, as she was a goddess of the last moments of birth. As the birth of Horus became more intimately associated with the resurrection of Osiris, so Heqet's role became one more closely associated with resurrection.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitos Osiris ia memberikan napas hidup kepada Horus saat lahir.",
        "en": "In the Osiris myth she breathes life into Horus at birth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heqet-c06",
      "source_id": "heqet-s2",
      "quote": "Heqet (Heket) - Goddess of fertility and childbirth, depicted as a frog or a woman with the head of a frog.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 79",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber pendamping menggambarkannya sebagai katak atau perempuan berkepala katak.",
        "en": "The accompanying source depicts her as a frog or frog-headed woman."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Seshat adalah dewi penulisan, kebijaksanaan, dan pengetahuan Mesir. Ia menjadi juru tulis dan pencatat, serta dipercaya menciptakan tulisan.",
      "en": "Seshat is the Egyptian goddess of writing, wisdom and knowledge. She acts as scribe and record keeper and is credited with inventing writing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "seshat-c01",
        "seshat-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia digambarkan membantu Khasekhemwy dalam ritual merentangkan tali. Teks Piramida memanggilnya Nyonya Para Pembangun. Seni memperlihatkan lambang berujung tujuh di atas kepalanya. Ia menjadi pelindung perpustakaan umum maupun pribadi.",
      "en": "She is depicted helping Khasekhemwy in the stretching-the-cord ritual. The Pyramid Texts call her Lady of Builders. Art shows a seven-pointed emblem above her head. She is patron of public and private libraries.",

      "id": "Sokar adalah dewa elang kawasan pemakaman Memphis dalam agama Mesir. Hubungannya dengan Ptah dan Osiris menghasilkan Ptah-Sokar-Osiris.",
      "en": "Sokar is a falcon god of the Memphite necropolis in Egyptian religion. His connection with Ptah and Osiris produces Ptah-Sokar-Osiris.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sokar-c01",
        "sokar-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia sering berupa elang bermumi atau kepala elang yang muncul dari bukit. Amduat menggambarkannya berdiri di atas ular di antara dua sayap. Kitab Orang Mati menyebutnya membuat mangkuk perak. Sumber pendamping menempatkan Sokar sebagai penjaga pintu masuk dunia bawah.",
      "en": "He is often a mummified hawk or a hawk’s head emerging from a mound. The Amduat shows him standing on a serpent between two wings. The Book of the Dead says he fashions silver bowls. The accompanying source places Sokar as guardian of the underworld entrance.",

    {
      "id": "sokar-s1",

    },
    {
      "id": "sokar-s2",
      "url": "https://www.worldhistory.org/article/885/egyptian-gods---the-complete-list/",
      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sokar-c01",
      "source_id": "sokar-s1",
      "quote": "is a hawk or falcon god of the Memphite necropolis in the Ancient Egyptian religion",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sokar adalah dewa elang kawasan pemakaman Memphis dalam agama Mesir.",
        "en": "Sokar is a falcon god of the Memphite necropolis in Egyptian religion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sokar-c02",
      "source_id": "sokar-s1",
      "quote": "Although the meaning of his name remains uncertain, the Egyptians in the Pyramid Texts linked his name to the anguished cry of Osiris to Isis 'Sy-k-ri' ('hurry to me'), or possibly skr, meaning \"cleaning the mouth\". In the underworld, Sokar is strongly linked with two other gods, Ptah the Creator god and chief god of Memphis, and Osiris the god of the dead.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hubungannya dengan Ptah dan Osiris menghasilkan Ptah-Sokar-Osiris.",
        "en": "His connection with Ptah and Osiris produces Ptah-Sokar-Osiris."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sokar-c03",
      "source_id": "sokar-s1",
      "quote": "Sokar was usually depicted as a mummified hawk and sometimes as a mound from which the head of a hawk appears. Here he is called 'he who is on his sand'. Sometimes, he is shown on his hennu barque which was an elaborate sledge for negotiating the sandy necropolis. One of his titles was 'He of Restau' which means the place of 'openings' or tomb entrances.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia sering berupa elang bermumi atau kepala elang yang muncul dari bukit.",
        "en": "He is often a mummified hawk or a hawk’s head emerging from a mound."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sokar-c04",
      "source_id": "sokar-s1",
      "quote": "In the New Kingdom Book of the Underworld, the Amduat, he is shown standing on the back of a serpent between two spread wings; as an expression of freedom this suggests a connection with resurrection or perhaps a satisfactory transit of the underworld.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Amduat menggambarkannya berdiri di atas ular di antara dua sayap.",
        "en": "The Amduat shows him standing on a serpent between two wings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sokar-c05",
      "source_id": "sokar-s1",
      "quote": "Sokar, possibly through his association with Ptah, has a connection with artisans. In the Book of the Dead, he is said to fashion silver bowls and at Tanis a silver coffin of Sheshonq II has been discovered decorated with the iconography of Sokar.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab Orang Mati menyebutnya membuat mangkuk perak.",
        "en": "The Book of the Dead says he fashions silver bowls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sokar-c06",
      "source_id": "sokar-s2",
      "quote": "guardian of the entrance to the underworld",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 187",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber pendamping menempatkan Sokar sebagai penjaga pintu masuk dunia bawah.",
        "en": "The accompanying source places Sokar as guardian of the underworld entrance."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Wepwawet adalah dewa jakal Mesir terkait pemakaman, perang, dan kerajaan, dengan pusat kultus di Asyut. Namanya berarti pembuka jalan.",
      "en": "Wepwawet is an Egyptian jackal deity of funerals, war and royalty, with a cult centre at Asyut. His name means opener of the ways.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wepwawet-c01",
        "wepwawet-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia membuka jalan melalui Duat bagi roh orang mati. Teks Piramida menempatkan kelahirannya di tempat suci Wadjet. Ia dapat digambarkan sebagai jakal hitam atau laki-laki berkepala jakal. Sumber pendamping mengaitkan Wepwawet-Ra dengan dewa matahari Ra.",
      "en": "He opens ways through the Duat for spirits of the dead. The Pyramid Texts place his birth in Wadjet’s sacred shrine. He may be depicted as a black jackal or jackal-headed man. The accompanying source connects Wepwawet-Ra with the sun god Ra.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wepwawet-c03",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wepwawet-c01",
      "source_id": "wepwawet-s1",
      "quote": "was originally a jackal deity of funerary rites, war, and royalty, whose cult centre was Asyut in Upper Egypt",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wepwawet adalah dewa jakal Mesir terkait pemakaman, perang, dan kerajaan, dengan pusat kultus di Asyut.",
        "en": "Wepwawet is an Egyptian jackal deity of funerals, war and royalty, with a cult centre at Asyut."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wepwawet-c02",
      "source_id": "wepwawet-s1",
      "quote": "His name means opener of the ways and he is often depicted as a wolf standing at the prow of a solar-boat.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berarti pembuka jalan.",
        "en": "His name means opener of the ways."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wepwawet-c03",
      "source_id": "wepwawet-s1",
      "quote": "Over time, the connection to war and thus to death led to Wepwawet also being seen as one who opened the ways to, and through, Duat, for the spirits of the dead. Through this, Wepwawet became associated with Anubis, a deity that was worshiped in Asyut, eventually being considered his brother.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membuka jalan melalui Duat bagi roh orang mati.",
        "en": "He opens ways through the Duat for spirits of the dead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wepwawet-c04",
      "source_id": "wepwawet-s1",
      "quote": "The Pyramid Texts state that Wepwawet was born in the Peru-nu, the sacred shrine of the goddess Wadjet, while an alternative myth suggests he emerged from a tamarisk bush. Consequently, Wepwawet is often confused with Anubis. This deity appears in the temple of Seti I at Abydos.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Piramida menempatkan kelahirannya di tempat suci Wadjet.",
        "en": "The Pyramid Texts place his birth in Wadjet’s sacred shrine."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wepwawet-c05",
      "source_id": "wepwawet-s1",
      "quote": "In Egyptian art, Wepwawet was depicted as a black jackal, or as a man with the head of a jackal. In the temple of Seti I at Abydos, Wepwawet appears to have grey-colored fur, though this is likely due to loss of pigmentation, as elsewhere in the temple, black paint is almost entirely faded. In rare cases, he appears in fully human form.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dapat digambarkan sebagai jakal hitam atau laki-laki berkepala jakal.",
        "en": "He may be depicted as a black jackal or jackal-headed man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wepwawet-c06",
      "source_id": "wepwawet-s2",
      "quote": "as Wepwawet-Ra, with the sun god Ra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 216",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber pendamping mengaitkan Wepwawet-Ra dengan dewa matahari Ra.",
        "en": "The accompanying source connects Wepwawet-Ra with the sun god Ra."
      }
    }

      "anat-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Anat adalah dewi perang dan perburuan yang dikenal dari teks Ugarit. Dalam Siklus Baal ia membantu Baal memperoleh kekuasaan dan membalaskan kematiannya.",
      "en": "Anat is a goddess of warfare and hunting known from Ugaritic texts. In the Baal Cycle she helps Baal obtain kingship and avenges his death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anat-c01",
        "anat-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Anat masuk ke Mesir pada Zaman Perunggu dan penting pada masa Ramses II. Kajian awal menganggapnya dewi kesuburan seksual, tetapi itu bukan lagi konsensus para ahli. Menurut teori Lambert, ia berasal dari tradisi Amori. Satu versi Contendings of Horus and Set menjadikannya pasangan Set atas saran Neith.",
      "en": "Anat enters Egypt in the Bronze Age and is prominent under Ramesses II. Early scholarship considers her a goddess of sexual fertility, but that is no longer scholarly consensus. According to Lambert’s theory, she originates in Amorite tradition. One version of the Contendings of Horus and Set makes her Set’s consort at Neith’s suggestion.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anat-c03",
        "anat-c04",
        "anat-c05",

      "title": "Egyptian Gods - The Complete List - World History Encyclopedia",
      "author": null,

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "anat-c01",
      "source_id": "anat-s1",
      "quote": "was a goddess associated with warfare and hunting, best known from the Ugaritic texts.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anat adalah dewi perang dan perburuan yang dikenal dari teks Ugarit.",
        "en": "Anat is a goddess of warfare and hunting known from Ugaritic texts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anat-c02",
      "source_id": "anat-s1",
      "quote": "In Ugarit, Anat was one of the main goddesses, and regularly received offerings, as attested in texts written both in the local Ugaritic language and in Hurrian. She also frequently appears in myths, including the Baal Cycle and the Epic of Aqhat.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Siklus Baal ia membantu Baal memperoleh kekuasaan dan membalaskan kematiannya.",
        "en": "In the Baal Cycle she helps Baal obtain kingship and avenges his death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anat-c03",
      "source_id": "anat-s1",
      "quote": "At some point in time in the Bronze Age, either during the reign of Hyksos or shortly after its end, Anat was introduced to Egypt, and achieved a degree of prominence during the reign of Ramesses II, whose devotion to her is well attested. Evidence for Egyptian worship of Anat is also available from various sites in Palestine which were controlled by the pharaohs in the Bronze Age.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anat masuk ke Mesir pada Zaman Perunggu dan penting pada masa Ramses II.",
        "en": "Anat enters Egypt in the Bronze Age and is prominent under Ramesses II."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anat-c04",
      "source_id": "anat-s1",
      "quote": "Anat was characterized as a fertility goddess associated with human sexuality in early scholarship, but despite the occasional modern support, this view is no longer the consensus among experts. Proposed etymologies of her name and interpretations of texts she appears in are a subject of criticism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kajian awal menganggapnya dewi kesuburan seksual, tetapi itu bukan lagi konsensus para ahli.",
        "en": "Early scholarship considers her a goddess of sexual fertility, but that is no longer scholarly consensus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anat-c05",
      "source_id": "anat-s1",
      "quote": "In the light of Lambert's theory, Anat, like her presumed forerunner Ḫanat, would be an Amorite deity in origin.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut teori Lambert, ia berasal dari tradisi Amori.",
        "en": "According to Lambert’s theory, she originates in Amorite tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anat-c06",
      "source_id": "anat-s2",
      "quote": "In one version of The Contendings of Horus and Set, she is given as a consort to Set at the suggestion of the goddess Neith.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 25",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu versi Contendings of Horus and Set menjadikannya pasangan Set atas saran Neith.",
        "en": "One version of the Contendings of Horus and Set makes her Set’s consort at Neith’s suggestion."
      }
    }
  ],
